## Summary

The Attention System is the simulation of the web's regard for the ship: one global, integer-only level that rises with the light the crew burns, the noise they make, the power they draw and the depth they work at, and falls back toward a depth-set baseline when they show restraint. Crossing one threshold starts a seeded, escalating 15 to 30 second **tell** (sound in a structure-borne bed, filaments resolving at the lamp cone's edge, never a meter) that restraint can abort; if it is not aborted, a **breach** puts STRESS on the section where the crew was loudest and on everything welded to it, sweeps the home-ship's single beacon, and ends in a short silence. It owns the tell-window contract: no web-originated harm lands outside a told breach, which is how Pillars 2 and 3 become testable.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer (designed), Life-Support Graph (designed), Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query (designed), Player Controller & Input (designed), Save/Load & Versioned Format (designed), Suit & Equipment (designed; soft, none at MVP)`; neighbours designed after this GDD (rows refreshed in the 2026-10-27 consistency pass where asked): `Ship Body & Interior Physics, Web Map & Transit, Horror Entities, Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Adaptive Audio & Tells (Designed, adaptive-audio-and-tells.md)`

## Overview

The Attention System is the web's regard of the ship, as simulation. In one sentence: it is a scene-tree-free, integer-only `SIM` block, `attention.state`, advanced by one divisor-6 step hook, `attention.step`, that holds **one global attention level** which rises with the light the crew keeps burning, the noise they make, the power they draw and the depth they work at, falls back toward a depth-set baseline when they show restraint, and drives a four-phase breach machine, **CALM → TELL → BREACH → SUBSIDING**, whose second step, the tell, is a promise to the player: a window of 10 to 30 seconds, seeded and fixed at the moment the level crosses the threshold, in which the world escalates and the player can still choose to be quiet.

**The data layer.** Stimuli reach the level by two paths and no others. *Pushed noise* arrives through one Class B mutator, `push_stimulus(kind, source_ref, class)`, from the Player Controller (gait classes), Interaction (tool and one-shot classes); Horror Entities never calls it (HE-36); Attention owns the unit table `U[class]`, and one walking tick of noise defines the unit (1 attention unit, AU). *Ordered reads* take light from the Emitter State's per-emitter list and power from the Life-Support Graph's `power_use(section_id)`; the Suit pushes nothing, and Attention never reads Audio. The level is held in milli-AU, integrates the three channels with one memory constant `TAU_ATT_TICKS`, and decays by a ceiling step toward the baseline `B`, so it always reaches `B` exactly. One threshold `ATT_THRESHOLD` (theta) with a hold band (theta_HOLD) starts and ends events. The machine's only writes to the world are two: M17 `deposit_trace(STRESS, ...)` at `BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT`, and the `set_beacon` command. Everything else it does is **publish** `attention.view`, a SIM read view of integers that Lighting, Audio, Horror, Sanity, Save/Load and its own presenter read.

**The player-facing effect.** The player never sees the level. They meet this system as the hull creaking, from everywhere and nowhere, and creaking again, a little sooner each time; as filaments at the very edge of their lamp's cone that were texture a moment ago and are now strands; as dust in the beam turning as if the air had been displaced. That is the **tell**, and its length is not shown. Inside it the player can do the one thing the web responds to, which is less: kill the chest lamp, set the cutter down, open the breaker, stand still. If the level falls under the hold band and stays there, the tell ends in nothing and the creaking stops (an abort). If it does not, the tell ends in a **breach**: the section where the crew was loudest, and every section welded to it, takes STRESS (worn hardware fails faster than a person can walk), the home-ship's single beacon sweeps red, and a short silence follows when it is over. The player can survive a breach, or leave it by pod; they cannot win it. Camera, controls, lamp and ambient light do not change in any phase.

**The contract this GDD owns.** Every web-originated harm to the ship, and provisionally to a crew body, must land inside a told breach: a tell of at least `TELL_MIN_TICKS` (derived as `ticks_for_seconds(W_MIN_WARNING_S)`) that the player could perceive through two independent senses and could have aborted by going dark. The Graph's fairness gate covers *endogenous* losses only (LS-13); for every non-player source it states "must therefore be preceded by that system's own Pillar 2 tell". This GDD is that tell. It is written as a testable predicate, `tell_window_contract`, audited by a `TellAudit` ledger that exists as a test fixture before Horror Entities and Adaptive Audio are designed, and it discharges the Graph's review criterion at LSG:1063.

**What the game loses without it.** The core loop's hinge ("Light, noise, power spikes and depth raise it; it is shown in the world; crossing a line flips the rhythm. Dread is feeling it come." — game concept, Core Loop) would be a flat spawn timer, which is the exact outcome Pillar 3's design test rejects, or a hidden threshold, which Pillar 2's design test rejects. Horror Entities would have no phase to act inside and no permission predicate to honour; Save/Load would have no tell to defer an autosave around; Lighting would have no tier to gutter on; Adaptive Audio would have no beat to sound; Sanity would have no true, unhideable event to measure a lie against. The core hypothesis (the two-rhythm boarding-and-breach loop is tense and absorbing for a 30-minute solo session) cannot be tested without it.

**MVP scope.** One threshold, one tier of lamp tell, one locus rule, one breach content (STRESS plus beacon plus published phase; RUPTURE and GOUGE are Horror's), one noise-only push API, ring-1 baseline plus placeholder rows for rings 2 and 3, and a contract-only suppressant with no effect verb. Multiple tiers, per-ring thresholds, the suppressant verb, per-player attribution and rate-coupled tell length are Vertical Slice addenda and are named, not absorbed (AT-30); the per-ring light and power stimulus gain is a pillar-flagged Vertical Slice requirement on Depth Rings & Scarcity (AT-6); phantom tells and false calm are rejected by pillar, permanently (AT-17).

**Index wording reconciled.** The index rationale (IDX Systems Enumeration bullet 17) is honoured with five corrections, each recorded as a refresh owed and never as an edit:

| The index says | This GDD does | Why | Refresh owed |
|---|---|---|---|
| noise and power stimuli "pushed in by Graph, Suit, Interaction, Assembly and Horrors" | **two intake paths**: noise is pushed (Controller, Interaction; Horror pushes none and is not a declared caller, HE-36); light and power are *ordered reads* (`emitters()`, `power_use`); Suit and Assembly push nothing at MVP | the Graph publishes `power_use` as a read (LS-19); Suit declares "No stimuli pushed" (SE-31); Interaction already pushes LOUD at cut completion (IA-33), so an Assembly push would double count | index bullet 17 wording (C6); kernel Interactions row 4 (C7) |
| "flicker as a Lighting modifier" | the lamp effect is a **gutter**, published as a tier | Lighting bans the word for the tell (LO-19, LO-20) | index wording (C10) |
| "Lighting never reads Attention" (IDX soft-cycle note) | Lighting reads only the published tier and timing; Attention reads nothing Lighting produces | LO-20 supersedes it | index soft-cycle note (C12) |
| "baseline set by depth" | `B = B_table[ring]` of the deepest section holding a living player; per-ring values belong to Depth Rings (VS) | one deterministic rule; "loudest player" is undefined | none |
| Suit listed as a Hard dependency | Suit is **soft, none at MVP**: contract text for the suppressant only | Suit pushes nothing and Attention reads nothing of it | index edge Attention → Suit (C14) |

**Reading guide.** Detailed Design holds the thirty-odd named rules `AT-1` to `AT-32` (Core Rules), the state machine, and one Interactions table per neighbour. Numbers in this document are `[PLACEHOLDER]` unless a registry name says otherwise; every locked constant (`TICK_RATE`, `W_MIN_WARNING_S`, `BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT`, `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS`, `NOISE_PER_TICK`, `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`, `MAX_EMITTERS`, `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP`, `AUTOSAVE_*`, `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`, `MOD_FLOOR_PM`) is cited by name and never restated with a number of this document's own. Lighting's **`gutter_recipe` bounds** (its variables `G_LO` and `G_HI`, tier 1 at 480 and 960 frames, REG:2187-2188, registry `gutter_recipe` variables) are not registry entries: they are cited as "Lighting's `gutter_recipe` bounds" and take no `referenced_by` from this document (C24).

## Player Fantasy

**It noticed.** The Synaptic Sea's web does not hunt you; it *looks*, and what it looks by is what you do. Every lamp you light, every loop you run, every running step and every cut through a hatch is something it can see. Depth sets how closely it is already watching. Your choices move it from there.

Most of the time you are in the boarding rhythm: still, dusty, deliberate, every door a decision about how loud to be. Then the hull creaks, and creaks again, from everywhere and nowhere. At the edge of your light, filaments that were only texture resolve into strands, and the dust in your beam turns as if something displaced the air. The creaks come closer together, and nothing tells you when they will stop. Your lamp, your hands and your view do not change. The ship does, and the web is leaning in. This is the tell: ten to thirty seconds that are yours.

In that window, restraint is a real move. Kill the lamp, set down the cutter, open the breaker, stand still, and the web can lose interest. The creaking stops and the strands sink back into texture. Or you decide the scrubber is worth it, finish the cut, and run for the pod while the section you stressed fails faster than you can walk. Leave, and the web lets go: the red beacon that swept the home-ship while the breach was on goes dark, and the wreck behind you goes on failing in silence. You did not win. You chose, and you can say afterwards what you heard and what you did about it.

The tell never lies. Your sanity can show you a monster in the dark. It cannot fake the creak that comes before the web turns on the ship or on you, and it cannot hide it. When harm arrives without that warning, the first thing to doubt is your eyes. *(Crew-body scope provisional: Horror Entities must repeat AT-15 or surface a CONFLICT.)*

**The emotion is being noticed, not being attacked.** The art bible names it: "It noticed. The small-animal feeling of being looked at. Not yet fear — attention." (ART 2.7). This system owns the gap between *noticed* and *fear*, and makes that gap the player's to spend. The power fantasy is the night-shift engineer's or the cave diver's: *I know what makes me loud, and I can choose to be quiet.* The player has no power over the web; their power is over their own emission. They never beat it. They go unnoticed, or they get through being noticed, or they leave.

**Framing choice (orchestrator ruling, logged).** Three framings were weighed. F1, *the web watches the ship* (direct, the pillar sentence itself), is adopted. F2, predator and prey, was rejected because it drifts toward hunter vocabulary (the combat anti-pillar) and toward Horror Entities' turf, and because it implies a hunted *body* when this system watches the *ship*. F3, ship as nervous system, was rejected because it collides with the art bible's stage-3 body imagery, which belongs to the sanity lie ("pipes read as veins"); the sane world must not speak the lie's language. Only F2's *affect* (ART 2.7's small-animal line) is kept.

**Pillar text served (verbatim).**

> **Pillar 3, The Web Watches.** "The players' light, noise, power use and restraint raise or lower its attention. Attention is shown in the world (creaking, flicker, filament movement), never as a hidden meter. Depth sets the baseline; player action moves it from there. Restraint and stealth are real strategies. *Design test*: a flat spawn timer vs. attention-driven escalation → attention."
>
> **Pillar 2, Dread Before Chaos.** "Every breach is preceded by a tell a careful player could have perceived (sound, light, gauge drift, hull stress). This pillar owns tempo and presentation; Pillar 3 owns causality. *Design test*: breach fires at a hidden threshold vs. breach preceded by a 10–30 second escalating tell → the tell."

*Lexical note.* The pillar's word "flicker" stays inside the quotation; the pillar text is not ours to edit. In every sentence this GDD writes, the lamp effect is a **gutter** (Lighting bans "flicker" for the tell, LO-19, LO-20). *Scope note.* P2's examples "gauge drift" and "hull stress" are not Attention tell channels at MVP: the real-gauge needle is dropped (C1, AT-17) and hull-stress hairlines are cut and routed to the art-director (AT-20). Attention's tell is sound (a creak beat in a structure-borne bed) and light (filaments resolving at the cone's edge, dust turning, an optional lamp gutter). Hull stress is the breach's *consequence* (STRESS), which is where P2's example lands.

**The design test sentence (this system's, paired with the pillars').** *A breach — or any web-originated harm — that lands without a full escalating tell the player could have perceived and acted on, vs. one that lands only after a tell the player could have aborted by going dark → the abortable tell.* In pillar form: **"a breach the player could not have prevented vs. a breach they chose not to prevent → the one they chose."** It has two machine-checkable halves. *Could have perceived:* `TellAudit` holds over every recorded run, and every legal `(tell_len, seed)` publishes at least `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` beats in the floor window, each sense alone sufficient. *Could have aborted:* the abortability invariant (AT-10) holds at every ring's survivable-dark floor. A tell that always ends in breach would be a timer in costume, which fails Pillar 3's design test; a tell the player physically cannot beat is the same thing.

**Anchor player moments (worked; numbers are the rulings' placeholders).**

*Moment A, "I can leave now" (the abort, the Pillar 3 payoff).* Derelict A, ring 1. The player is partway through a salvage cut with two fixtures lit from a breaker they closed earlier. The level crosses `ATT_THRESHOLD`; a seeded tell begins. Beat 0 is a creak in the bed, audio first. Over the next beats two or three filaments at the cone's edge resolve into strands, and the dust turns. The player releases the channel (the partial is kept for `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`), kills the chest lamp, opens the breaker and stands still in the dark. The level falls under theta_HOLD and stays there for `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS`, or is under it at `breach_due_T`. Phase returns to CALM: no beacon, no STRESS, the beat stops. With the lamp off and the breaker open no lit region remains, so no filament is drawn at all (ART 3.3 / Principle 1). *Two outcomes, both fair.* In the favourable case the abort lands inside `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` and the player relights and resumes the cut. In the common case the partial has already lapsed and the cut restarts from zero. An abort usually costs the partial; that is the price of restraint, chosen on purpose. *What the player learns:* the web is causal and restraint is a move.

*Moment B, "We chose to be loud" (commit, breach, escape).* Same setup, but the scrubber is worth it. The player keeps cutting through the tell and finishes; cut completion is a one-shot LOUD push (IA-33). At `breach_due_T` the level is still at or above theta_HOLD, and the breach commits. Derelict A's locus section and every section of its body take STRESS. A worn pipe that was ticking toward failure now fails faster; its trail outruns the player's walk. There is no beacon on A (the world's single beacon is on the home-ship): the creak bed, the trails and the noise of failing hardware are the alarm. They carry the scrubber to the pod. **Pod-hop departure from derelict A ends the breach** *(provisional on Web Map & Transit)*: they escaped, they did not win. The machine moves to SUBSIDING and the home-ship beacon switches off. In co-op the crew at home saw the beacon sweep red from breach entry until that departure (the true global fact "a breach is on"); the escaping player arrives to a dark beacon and silence. Derelict A's STRESS lapses on the Graph's own hold, after the alarm has stopped. *Variant, home-ship locus:* there is no escape by pod; the breach runs until `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` or until the crew goes quiet for `CLEAR_HOLD_TICKS` after `BREACH_MIN_TICKS`; welded derelict sections share it, so a welded derelict is not a safe harbour. *What the player learns:* the breach was a price they chose with full warning, not an ambush. They can tell the story afterwards ("we heard it creaking and kept cutting"). Death in a breach is lethal (user decision: "death is a bitch. It happens.") and fair, because it was told.

*Moment C, "The lights we left on" (off-screen causality; Web Map dormancy applies, WM:102).* The player leaves derelict A's fixtures lit and pods home. "Off-screen and unoccupied emitters count" (EM-27), so the level keeps creeping while they weld at home. A tell starts; the non-positional bed reaches them though the locus is A's section; the home-ship beacon sweeps at breach entry; A's sections take the STRESS, and the home-ship does only if A is welded to it. Under LS-17's default (a left-behind derelict is dormant, DA-13 (a); Web Map adopts it, WM:102) this shrinks to "lights left on in a section of your own welded ship", which still holds. *What the player learns:* the web sees the ship, not the body. Leaving lights on is a decision with a delayed cost.

*Moment ordering guard (the playtest tripwire for commit-dominance).* Moments A and B must both occur within the MVP 30-minute session for a typical player. If playtests show only A (never a breach) or only B (nobody aborts, or can never abort), the tuning is wrong, not the fantasy. Only B is the failure AT-11 names: a breach that costs too little against an abort that costs the partial makes commit dominant, empties P3's restraint and teaches players to ignore tells. Only A is the opposite failure: restraint is free and the breach is never met. The Feel Acceptance Criteria and AC-AT-80 carry the measurable form.

**Emotional arc across one cycle (never a meter).**

| Phase (`attention.view.phase`) | Player feels | What the world does | What the player can do |
|---|---|---|---|
| **CALM** (boarding) | forensic curiosity under a held breath (ART 2.6); every door a decision, part of it how loud | dust drifts in the beam; "nothing else moves unless the player moves it"; filaments are texture at the cone's edge; the player's own run footsteps are audibly distinct from walking (PCI:135), the only noise readout. Above `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM` the world stirs in `CUE_STEPS` quantised steps: ordinary hull-settling creaks a little more frequent, a few more filaments at the rim. Never a continuous mapping of `level_pm` | spend light, noise and power deliberately; weigh a module "against the noise of taking it" |
| **TELL** (`tell_len` from `TELL_MIN_TICKS` to `TELL_MAX_TICKS`, seeded at entry) | *it noticed*: prickling, then leaning in | beat 0 in the entry run, audio first; on every beat one creak in the structure-borne bed, audible to every living player, and one filament-resolve in every lit region; spacing and density rise with elapsed time, not with progress, so nothing says when it ends; fixtures and lanterns may gutter (never the cryo emergency lamp); beam, ambient and camera do not change | **decide**: kill the lamp, release the channel, open a breaker, stand still, leave; sustained restraint aborts; or commit |
| **BREACH** | controlled panic (ART 2.8): "more things moving, not the screen fighting the player" | STRESS on the locus body; the home-ship beacon sweeps red; horrors may act inside the window (Horror's GDD, provisional); the level keeps integrating, so staying loud keeps it going up to `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` | patch, re-route, go quiet, or leave (pod-hop departure from a non-home locus body ends it) |
| **SUBSIDING**, then refractory | *then silence* (LSG:986: "A long tell, a spreading wrongness, a short collapse, then silence") | beacon off; no new STRESS; the last deposit lapses on the Graph's hold, so the wrongness may outlast the alarm; filaments sink back; creaks thin | repair; the level is cut to a residual, not wiped, so a player still loud at the end of the refractory tips straight into a new tell |

*Flow note.* CALM is long and flow-friendly; TELL is a deliberate flow break that hands the player a decision; BREACH is high-arousal recovery; SUBSIDING plus the refractory is the pacing valley. The valley is gated by machine state and published in the read view; it is not a hidden scheduler.

**How the fantasy deepens under hallucination (the tell is a truth channel, AT-17).** Nothing Attention publishes or presents is added, removed, delayed or altered by the Perception Filter or by Sanity, and Attention reads no sanity and no `PERCEPTION_OUT` value. This makes the tell one of Pillar 4's surviving channels of truth and gives low-sanity play a sharp verification tool.

1. *"No tell, no harm from the web."* One predicate (AT-15) is audited over every web-originated M17 RUPTURE/GOUGE/STRESS, every environmental M18 EXOGENOUS and, provisionally, every lethal act by a horror on a crew body. So when a phantom entity lunges (hallucination class a) or a false "hull failing" state appears (class b) with no creak beat, no resolving filaments and no beacon before it, the player has a cheap first check that costs attention: *the web didn't look up, so this isn't the web.* It is only as good as the listening behind it (you must have been listening to the bed), and listening is a spend, an ear kept on the structure instead of on the work. The check rules out web-originated **harm**, never an entity's **presence**: a thing standing in the dark in CALM may still be real, so "an entity seen in CALM is safe to ignore" is false by design. The boundary is honest: player-originated harm (a cut, a vent, a hallucinating crewmate's tool) needs no tell, and Horror's own stalk is additive, so the check rules out the web, not your crew. Friendly harm stays Sanity's gate and the human channel stays the way to verify a crewmate. *Routed must-considers.* To **Sanity & Hallucination Rules:** phantom entities should be able to co-occur with real tells and breaches, where they are hardest to separate from the real thing. To **Horror Entities:** a non-lethal presence or stalk outside BREACH is required, so CALM is never known-safe (AT-15 gates lethal acts only; without this the pair would hollow hallucination class (a) and the horror).
2. *Reading the rim.* Attention's filaments resolve at the cone's edge in the web's iridescence (teal → green → bronze → gold, ART 4.4). Sanity stage 2 puts a violet fringe at the same rim. The rule players learn: *iridescence at the rim is the web, and it is true; violet at the rim is you, and it is a lie.* The two must differ by more than hue under colourblind modes and the luminance ladder (shape: branching strands against a continuous fringe; motion: resolving on the beat against a static tint). Routed to the art-director (CR-RIM).
3. *Hidden real entities still leave Attention's footprint.* "The hallucination hides the body, never the trail" (game concept, Pillar 4(a)): a low-sanity player who sees nothing in the dark still hears the bed.
4. *The beam is never touched.* Radius, temperature and shape do not change in any phase (ART 2.7), and a crewmate's light is still the crewmate.
5. *Closed by pillar, permanently.* False calm (Sanity suppressing a real tell) and phantom tells are rejected, not deferred (gate ruling, CD-GDD-ALIGN). A false calm hides a real tell, which produces exactly the ambush Pillar 2 forbids; a phantom tell spends the player's restraint on a lie and voids `TellAudit`. The tell is a standing truth channel in every tier. Sanity keeps its own disjoint sound family and its own rim fringe (item 2).

**Reference techniques (mechanic level; adopt or reject).**

| Reference | Technique | Verdict | Exactly what is taken or refused |
|---|---|---|---|
| Project Zomboid | noise attracts; consequence shown only in the world | **adopt** | noise as the main moment-to-moment stimulus with a long memory (`TAU_ATT_TICKS`); no HUD sound radius; loudness learned from your own footsteps; lethal, unforgiving consequence (user decision) |
| Alien: Isolation | audio-first approach (vents bang before it drops) | **adopt the ordering** | beat 0 is audio, published in the TELL-entry run |
| Alien: Isolation | motion tracker | **reject as a display** | no device or HUD shows Attention, including accessibility settings (ART 7.0, 7.6) |
| Thief | light gem | **reject** | a visibility readout; visibility is read from your own cone and the fixtures you powered |
| Amnesia: The Dark Descent | sanity readout plus darkness penalty | **reject both** | Attention is not sanity; darkness is not punished by Attention, it is priced by the lit-only verb rule (IA-6) |
| Darkest Dungeon | stress bar | **reject** | an on-screen meter |
| Subnautica | distant leviathan call; depth-gated danger | **adopt both** | the bed as "heard everywhere, located nowhere"; the depth-set baseline as the depth gradient |
| Stellaris | dread from systems, not cutscenes | **adopt** | no scripted breach; the onboarding arm obeys the tell floor; no authored exception |
| Barotrauma | hull failure you watch propagate | **adopt for BREACH** | STRESS makes endogenous failures propagate faster (LS-25); the breach is the ship failing, not a red screen |
| Pandorum / Beyond the Aquila Rift | wrongness arriving in a dead ship, leaking at the edge | **adopt the tone** | the tell is at the edge; the beam interior is identical, contrast rises because the rim gets busy; the chest-mounted hands-free lamp is untouched |
| Left 4 Dead "AI Director" | hidden pacing timer scheduling threats | **reject** | fails Pillar 3's design test; the refractory is a published, state-gated valley, not a scheduler |
| Resident Evil 7 / Outlast | heartbeat, breathing, screen shake as dread | **reject** | "the ship changes, you don't" (PCI:135, PIPE:151) |

**Anti-pillar and neighbour guard rails the fantasy must not break.** (1) *Not a combat game:* Attention spawns nothing and defines no creature; the suppressant is a contract only, worded "repel or distract" and never "fight" or "kill"; a breach is survived or escaped, never won. (2) *Not intentional PvP:* Attention never gates or enables friendly harm; noise is attributed to sections for the locus only, never shown as blame, and no "who made the noise" readout exists. (3) *Not a freeform builder:* no placement bonus or penalty beyond what placed modules emit. (4) *No authored story, no win state:* no scripted breach; decay returns the level to the baseline and never below, and the survivable-dark floor holds at every ring, so a dark ship can always end a tell. (5) *The ship changes, you don't:* no adrenaline, speed, noise or control change in any phase (PCI:135, PCI:1010) and no camera reaction, shake or zoom (PIPE:151); the prose never says "your heart races", "tunnel vision" or "time slows". (6) *Nothing shows the level:* the UI Requirements section reads "none, by decision". (7) *The end of the tell is unreadable.* (8) *The sane palette:* iridescence only, never flesh or violet. (9) *Two channels, each sufficient alone;* a deaf player with the lamp on and a low-vision player each have one. (10) *Restraint must be real and must not be free:* going dark costs verbs (lit-only, IA-6), air and heat (the loops shut off), time (`TAU_ATT_TICKS` memory, `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`) and a breaker's inrush (LSG:809).

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

*Conventions.* Rules are named `AT-n`. Each states a **Rule**, a **Criterion** (acceptance-criterion ids `AC-AT-nn`, written in Acceptance Criteria; the tell-window ones are written first, as the index requires) and a **Pillar**. Citations name the counterpart document and section; line numbers (`LSG:144`, `EM:247`, `IA:276`, `SL:528`, `ART:741`) were verified against the files on disk on 2026-10-26 and are given for convenience. **Units.** A *run* is one execution of `attention.step` (6 ticks at `TICK_RATE`, the Graph's `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`); the level is stored in **mAU** (milli-attention-units, 64-bit integer); **1 AU is one walking tick of noise** (defined by the Controller-owned registry row `NOISE_PER_TICK`, cited, never restated with a number here). All durations are ticks. Every new number below is a `[PLACEHOLDER]`, tuned in `assets/data/attention/`; the ruling that fixed its form is named in parentheses. **Disk state at authoring (Glob of `design/gdd/`, 2026-10-26, re-run at the part 2 pass: 17 files including this one):** designed and read: Command & Simulation Layer, Life-Support Graph, Player Controller & Input, Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query, Suit & Equipment, Save/Load & Versioned Format, Interaction System, 2D Lighting & Occlusion, Item & Module Database, Failure & Trail, Ship Section & Layout Data Model, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera. Two neighbours are on disk with status `In Design` and are **siblings in flight**, so they are cited by section id first and their line numbers drift (every `BDY:` and `DA-` line below is as of the 21:21 write and must be re-grepped): **Ship Body & Interior Physics** (`ship-body-and-interior-physics.md`) and **Derelict Authoring (Handmade Kits)** (`derelict-authoring-handmade-kits.md`; DA-24: exactly one BEACON in the world, on `home_ship` in `spine_corridor`, `node_ref` = `bcn_main`, a dedicated LOAD node (`mod_lamp`, 20 W, DA-24; OQ-DA-9 closed 'no sharing'), lint `BEACON_COUNT` (ERROR, BOOT), none on derelicts A or B; DA-23: `fx_cryo_lamp` is an EMERGENCY fixture, self-powered (`node_ref` 0) and never modulated by Attention's tell). No file exists for Horror Entities, Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Adaptive Audio & Tells, Web Map & Transit, Modular Ship Assembly, Perception Filter, Boarding & Salvage, HUD & Suit Layer, In-World Instruments, Chart Table or Debug Overlay: every contract with those is **provisional** and carries a *must repeat* clause.

#### Group A — Identity and scope

**AT-1. Block and hook identity.** *Rule.* One `SIM` block `attention.state` and one step hook `attention.step`. Both ids are new; the hook's `after` ids already exist, so an unknown id cannot fail boot.

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| hook | `(hook_id attention.step, owner attention, divisor 6, offset 0, after [graph.propagate.power, emitter.place])` |
| why offset 0 | LS-8: a reader shares its writer's offset (LSG:104). The kernel's stagger example "Graph 0, Attention 2, Suit 4" (KRN Formulas, D.6 Output Range) is a refresh owed (C3); it is not edited here |
| ordering | `emitter.place` is divisor 1, so a hook declaring `after: [emitter.place]` sees the current tick's emitters (EM-25); `graph.propagate.power` (root) is the Graph's power phase, so `power_use` is this step's. Suit's `suit.vitals` precedes the hook transitively (`suit.vitals → controller.step → body.integrate → emitter.place → attention.step`), so no `after: suit.vitals` is declared: Attention reads no Suit state at MVP |
| block | `class SIM`, `persist` true, `persist_scope WORLD`, `scope SHARED`, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, `schema_version` 1, `supported_versions [1]`, `restore_after [graph.state, emitter.state]` (section ids resolve in `post_restore`) |
| restore | SL-15 in full: two-phase `restore` returning `(ok, reason, detail)`; closed reason set `ATTENTION_RESTORE_{SCHEMA, RANGE, PHASE_INCOHERENT, SECTION_UNKNOWN, RNG_COUNTER}`; an out-of-range value is a hard failure and is **never clamped**; mutators track dirty keys explicitly (Godot 4.7: a packed-array element write does not call the property setter); a restore round trip is idempotent |
| persisted fields | `level_mAU`, `phase`, `tell_epoch`, `tell_start_T`, `tell_len`, `breach_due_T`, `tell_beat`, `beat_next_off`, `below_hold_runs`, `clear_runs`, `breach_enter_T`, `subside_end_T`, `refract_until_T`, `rearm_until_T`, `locus_section_id`, `locus_body` (the section set recorded at TELL entry), `locus_occupants_at_entry` (the living seats inside `locus_body` at BREACH entry), `tell_outcome`, `rng_counter`, `pending_mAU[section]`, `ledger_mAU[section]`, `held_ring`, `stress_next_burst_run`, `stress_pending_cursor`, `beacon_commanded`, `beacon_retry_left`. The pending accumulator, the ledger, the RNG counter and every timer persist (G4 save-equivalence). Section-keyed arrays are stored sorted by `section_id` |
| dirty keys | `ATT/L` (level) and `ATT/P` (phase block). The level changes every run, so presentation **polls** `attention.view`; the notification is not treated as an event |

*Criterion.* AC-AT-62 (hook and block registration; AC-AT-01 is the tell-window contract and is written first), AC-AT-22 (G4 mid-tell snapshot), AC-AT-23 (no reroll). *Pillar.* P2, P3 (serves P1 through truth). *Decided by:* R1; KRN CR-3, CR-8; LSG:104; EM-25.

**AT-2. One level, a ledger only to choose the locus.** *Rule.* There is **one** attention level `L` for the world: the web watches the ship, not a body. A per-section `ledger_mAU` exists only to choose the locus (AT-14); it decays with the same `TAU_ATT_TICKS` as `L`, so the locus reflects *recent* contribution and a section lit for an hour does not win forever. Noise carries a `source` PlayerId tag so Vertical Slice attribution is a data change, not a rewrite. Dormant (unloaded) sections contribute nothing and hold no ledger entry (LSG:174, LS-17). A per-section level, a per-player level and a second machine were weighed and rejected: they multiply Horror's work, break Save/Load's single `tell_remaining`, and exceed "MVP: one threshold".
*Criterion.* AC-AT-02. *Pillar.* P3. *Decided by:* R2; game concept MVP definition.

#### Group B — Intake: two paths, no others

**AT-3. Path 1, push: noise, one mutator, class-based.** *Rule.* `push_stimulus(kind, source_ref, class)` is a published **Class B mutator** (clamp, never reject; the kernel's CR-9 path a). `kind` is `NOISE` at MVP; other values are reserved and rejected by lint. `class ∈ {QUIET, WALK, RUN, CARRY, TOOL, LOUD}`. An unknown class is a counted no-op. It is callable only inside a `WriteScope` by the declared callers, the Controller and Interaction (Horror Entities pushes no noise, HE-36, and is not a declared caller). A push adds `U[class] × 1000` mAU to `pending_mAU` keyed by its source; the section is resolved at integration time (AT-14). **Attention owns the unit table `U[class]`; the Controller and Interaction own the *classes*.**

| Class | `U[class]` (AU per push) | Owner of the number | Pushed by | Cadence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `QUIET` | 0 | Attention | Interaction | never adds; counted |
| `WALK` | cited from registry `NOISE_PER_TICK` (walk) | Controller | Controller | each moving tick |
| `RUN` | cited from `NOISE_PER_TICK` (run) | Controller | Controller | each moving tick, ramp ticks included (PCI:560) |
| `CARRY` | cited from `NOISE_PER_TICK` (carry) | Controller | Controller | each moving tick |
| `TOOL` | `U_TOOL` placeholder 4 | Attention | Interaction | once per *accrued* channel tick; a tick that aborts before accrual pushes nothing (IA-33) |
| `LOUD` | `U_LOUD` placeholder 180 (= 30 × 6) | Attention | Interaction | one-shot at cut completion, seam-cut DONE, vent apply, throw landing |

Three relations are handed to Attention by Interaction and **adopted** (IA-33, hand-over I-8, Interaction OQ 13; registry `noise_push`, `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`) and become registered formulas and lint criteria: **IR-1** `2 ≤ U_TOOL ≤ 6`; **IR-2** `U_LOUD ≥ 30 × U_TOOL` (180 holds across the *whole* legal `U_TOOL` range, not only at the default); **IR-3** `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS ≤ 2 × TAU_ATT_TICKS`. A breaker close pushes nothing and a door motor pushes nothing (IA-33); the Graph's inrush enters as a *power* stimulus through `power_use` (AT-4). A push that lands after `attention.step` in the same tick, by topological order, is integrated on the next run; this is deterministic, so no `after: interaction.*` is declared. **Noise timing (orchestrator ruling: the ruling-time wording "the Controller keeps pushing from the previous tick's `moved`" is superseded by Ship Body & Interior Physics 4.1 on disk).** Ship Body (its read table for `moved(actor_id)` and its Cross-References refresh row "Controller / Attention", cited by section id first, as BDY-19 and BDY 4.1 (that row's own rule column; 4.1 is open with the Controller as owner); sibling in flight, lines drift: at 23:14 the Dependents row is `BDY:344` and the refresh row is `BDY:988`, at the 21:21 write `BDY:341` and `BDY:985`, at the 21:10 checkpoint `:497` and `:936`) does *not* adopt a persisted `moved`; it offers `moved(actor_id)` readable only from a hook ordered `after: [body.integrate]`, and the Controller owner chooses between a second divisor-1 Controller hook and another reader. Attention is indifferent: a zero- or one-tick lag is below its six-tick resolution, and the sum Attention sees per run is the Controller's per-tick pushes of up to six ticks (0 to 24 AU per run for one runner, PCI:560). **Attention repeats Body's text and accepts either** (Ship Body CP-3, 8.3 row "Controller / Attention"): running noise uses `moved` only from a hook ordered after `body.integrate` (the Controller's chosen hook); Attention reads `actor_section` and `body_of_actor` only, from `attention.step`, which is ordered transitively after `body.integrate` (`suit.vitals → controller.step → body.integrate → emitter.place → attention.step`). The Controller's change from "pushes a number" to "pushes a gait class" (its registry row resolves the class through `NOISE_PER_TICK`; its D.13 maps `loco_state` to the class) is a small refresh owed to the Controller (C9).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-03, AC-AT-04, AC-AT-05 (lint IR-1 to IR-3), AC-AT-06a to AC-AT-06e (a 180-tick patch pushes exactly 720,000 mAU; the AC-IA-107 stub). *Pillar.* P3. *Decided by:* R3; IA:645 ("class, never units"); registry `NOISE_PER_TICK`: "Attention's intake defines the units".

**AT-4. Path 2, ordered read: light and power.** *Rule.* At each run, after `emitter.place` and `graph.propagate.power`, Attention reads (a) `emitters()` for every loaded section (id, kind, `section_id`, `output_pm`, `reach_px`), and (b) `power_use(section_id)` per loaded section. It makes **no** `probe()` call, so `MAX_PROBES_PER_TICK` is unused (the registry note already says Attention does not read it). Light is a weighted sum of each emitter's term, where the inner term is the Emitter State's own `section_emission` per-emitter term (EM D.12), **cited by name, never re-derived here**. Weights are per emitter kind, in one dimension (micro-AU per unit per tick), placeholders:

| Kind (registry `emitter_kind`, closed list) | `W_L` | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `BEAM` (the owner's chest beam) | 1000 | counted **once**, from the emitter list at apex value; Attention never reads `lux_pm` as a stimulus and never adds the beam a second time (EM-21, EM-27) |
| `FIXTURE`, `NODE_LANTERN` | 1000 | the cryo emergency fixture counts as light (a real emitter) but is never a tell carrier (LO-20) |
| `DEPLOYABLE` (glowsticks at MVP) | 1000 | light is a spend, never a safe state (EM:722) |
| `FAULT_GLOW` | 1000 in CALM and TELL; **0** in BREACH and SUBSIDING | a breach's own fault glows never prolong it |
| `BEACON` | **0** (lint: always 0) | the alarm must not feed itself |

Power: `I_power = W_P × max(0, draw_w − lamps_lit_w) + W_S × max(0, delta_w_last5)`, with `noisy_w` reserved at weight 0 and flagged (placeholders `W_P` 100, `W_S` 6000 micro-AU per W per tick). **`served_pm` is not a power stimulus:** it already reaches Attention as light through `output_pm` (registry `power_allocation`), and using it again would double count. **Routed ask to the Graph owner (C11, confirmed by evidence):** the four `power_use` fields are named once (LS-19) and never defined by a formula; `noisy_w` has no definition anywhere; and LSG Example C states the inrush "1200 W spike in `power_use.delta_w_last5`" where the 1200 W includes 300 W of lamps while `lamps_lit_w` is reported separately. So `delta_w_last5` appears to *include* lamp watts, and a lamp switch-on would be a bounded five-step double count against EM-27 (the example is 54 AU of 216). This is flagged and routed, not silently accepted: the Graph owner is asked to define each field (semantics, W per step, sign, window) and to state whether `delta_w_last5` excludes `lamps_lit_w`; if it cannot, Attention subtracts the lamp delta itself. **Beacon exclusion (CP-C3).** The commanded beacon is a Graph LOAD on its own node `bcn_main` (`mod_lamp`, 20 W; AT-18, DA-24), so in **BREACH and SUBSIDING** Attention excludes its watts from **both** power terms: `W_P` is applied to `max(0, draw_w − lamps_lit_w − beacon_w)` and `W_S` to `max(0, delta_w_last5 − beacon_step_w)`, where `beacon_w` is the beacon's declared load while `beacon_commanded` is on (and the step of its switch-on or switch-off for the five steps after each). The C11 ask to the Graph and Emitter owners is extended by two questions: does `lamps_lit_w` include the beacon's `mod_lamp` node `bcn_main`, and does `delta_w_last5` include its 20 W step? If the answers make the exclusion redundant, the subtraction is a no-op. If `power_use` is absent, `I_power` is 0 for that section and one diagnostic is logged per process. The **Suit pushes nothing** (SE-31); **Assembly pushes nothing at MVP**, because Interaction already pushes LOUD at cut completion and a second push would double count; no `LOUD` is pushed at weld completion by anyone (Modular Ship Assembly T14 C5; IA-33). **Attention never reads Audio** (lint; the player's footsteps enter only as the Controller's pushed gait class).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-07, AC-AT-08, AC-AT-09, AC-AT-10, AC-AT-63. *Pillar.* P3, P1. *Decided by:* R5, R6, R25; EM:247 ("Attention owns units and weights"; "Off-screen and unoccupied emitters count"); LSG:794, LSG:809.

#### Group C — The level, the baseline, the threshold

**AT-5. The integrator: one level, one memory constant (a rate detector).** *Rule.* Each run: the three channel inputs are summed (`I_run = I_noise + I_light + I_power`), a **ceiling decay** toward the baseline `B` is subtracted, and the result is clamped to `[B, L_CEIL]`. There is one memory, `TAU_ATT_TICKS` (placeholder 1800: a job split across `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` then retains about 72 percent). There is **no separate spike term**: the power inrush enters as ordinary input over its at most five steps, because a second memory would contradict Interaction's single `tau_att`. The ceiling decay reaches `B` *exactly* (the integer truncation stall of LSG:464 cannot occur). Calibration placeholders: `ATT_THRESHOLD` (theta) 6000 AU, so continuous running with the beam on crosses theta in tens of seconds and continuous walking settles near 30 percent of theta; the derivation is in Formulas.
**ADDITION X1 (adopted by checkpoint ruling CQ-2; APPROVED at the CD-GDD-ALIGN gate): `L_CEIL`.** `L_CEIL = ATT_THRESHOLD × L_CEIL_PM / 1000`, placeholder `L_CEIL_PM` 1020. The ruled abortability invariant (AT-10 b) is stated for "the highest possible entry level, theta plus one run's input", but the entry level is not bounded by that: during the refractory and rearm windows the level keeps integrating and a runner can reach several times theta before the next legal tell, and one run's maximum input has no design bound (96 emitters alone are over 200 AU per run at weight 1000). Saturating the level at `L_CEIL` is the only way for the proof to exist, and it is what makes the abortability proof, and so P2's "window of agency", true at every entry level (the gate's reason for approving it). It changes nothing below theta.
**Stance (so no later pass "fixes" it): the level is a short-memory rate detector, not a session score.** With a memory of tens of seconds, a TELL means "you have been loud or lit at a high rate for roughly half a minute to a minute", never "you have done much in total". That makes causality local and legible (P3) and is why pacing comes from behaviour and not from drift. A longer `TAU_ATT_TICKS` is the only lever that makes a whole sortie matter, and it is capped from above by abortability (AT-10).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-11, AC-AT-12, AC-AT-13, AC-AT-14, AC-AT-57a to AC-AT-57g (calibration traces). *Pillar.* P3. *Decided by:* R4; IA:221 and :321 (one `tau_att`).

**AT-6. Baseline by depth.** *Rule.* `B = B_table[ring_B]`, where `ring_B` is the **maximum ring over sections that contain a living player** (a deterministic rule; "loudest player" is rejected as undefined). `ring_of(section_id)` is the registry formula `ring_of` (Web Map & Transit): the owning record's ring, a welded section resolving to the HOME ring, a parked pod to the ring of the record at `pod_at`, a pod mid-leg to the departure record's ring (equal to `held_ring`, AT-22); an absent or sub-1 ring resolves to 1, logged once per process. While a player is `IN_POD` the ring of the departure section is held (`held_ring`) until arrival. MVP ships ring 1 plus placeholder rows for rings 2 and 3 for tests; per-ring values belong to Depth Rings & Scarcity (Vertical Slice). A ring change that raises `B` above `L` lifts `L` to `B` at once; one that lowers `B` lets `L` decay toward the new floor. Two invariants are written as lint and automated criteria over the data: (i) `B_table` is non-decreasing with ring; (ii) the **survivable-dark floor**: at every ring, the dark steady level `x_dark` (the baseline plus everything the player cannot switch off, with the player still and the beam off) must satisfy **both** bounds. *The trivial steady bound* (R7 ii as ruled): `x_dark ≤ theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN`. *The binding bound* (F-15, AT-10 b): the decay from `L_CEIL` at that floor must reach `theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN` before `TELL_MIN_TICKS − 6` ticks elapse, which caps `x_dark` far below the trivial bound (about 1,253 AU at the placeholders, against 4,750 AU for the trivial one). **The unswitchable set** is named: (1) the cryo EMERGENCY lamp (`fx_cryo_lamp`, self-powered, DA-23), (2) `FAULT_GLOW` emitters (counted in CALM and TELL; a latent fault the player cannot extinguish), and (3) `DARK_FLOOR_LOAD_W`, the home-ship's minimum survival load (O2, heat, reactor) with lamps off, through the power channel. The lint re-runs whenever Assembly, Authoring or the Graph changes that set. This is what guarantees that restraint can always end a tell and that no depth makes a breach unavoidable. **Consequence stated honestly (risk RK-2):** at the ruled memory and hold band the invariant caps the deep-ring baseline at a small fraction of theta, so on the Attention axis P3's "Depth sets the baseline" and P5's "Deeper Is Richer" are almost inert; that is acceptable for a ring-1-only MVP and not beyond it. **Pillar-flagged Vertical Slice REQUIREMENT (gate ruling, CD-GDD-ALIGN; no longer a proposal):** a per-ring stimulus gain on the light and power channels only, never applied to noise (footsteps are the player's only noise readout and its scale must not change with depth), which Depth Rings & Scarcity must satisfy or surface as a CONFLICT. Vertical Slice acceptance criterion: AC-AT-119.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-15, AC-AT-16, AC-AT-64 (`abortability_play`). *Pillar.* P5, P3. *Decided by:* R7; IDX bullet 17 ("baseline set by depth"), Systems Enumeration row for Depth Rings.

**AT-7. One threshold, one hold band.** *Rule.* One threshold `theta = ATT_THRESHOLD`, identical for every ring at MVP, plus a hold band `theta_HOLD = theta × HOLD_PM / 1000` (placeholder `HOLD_PM` 800). TELL entry requires **all** of: phase CALM, `L ≥ theta`, `T ≥ refract_until_T`, and `T ≥ rearm_until_T` (the guards are inclusive, so entry exactly at a timer boundary is legal). Per-ring thresholds are a Vertical Slice addendum. **(Orchestrator ruling: R8's absolute definition of theta_HOLD stands.)** The game-designer's proposal GD-3 defines the hold line relative to the baseline instead (`B + HOLD_PM × (theta − B) / 1000`) because the absolute line lengthens the descent when `B` is large; the two are close at ring 1. The question is carried to Open Questions; it is moot while the baseline stays under the cap that AT-6 (ii) and AT-10 (b) impose, and the lint over every ring row is the guard.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-17. *Pillar.* P3. *Decided by:* R8; game concept ("MVP: one threshold").

#### Group D — The tell

**AT-8. The tell window: length and the floor (contract part 1).** *Rule.* At TELL entry, `tell_len = rng_int(TELL_DRAW_LO, TELL_DRAW_HI)` (inclusive), drawn with `rng_derive(world_seed, "attention.tell", rng_counter)`, then `rng_counter` increments; the counter lives in the block, so a restore cannot reroll the length (no reroll oracle). `TELL_MIN_TICKS` is **derived, not a knob**: `ticks_for_seconds(W_MIN_WARNING_S)`. `TELL_MAX_TICKS` is fixed by Pillar 2's text ("10–30 seconds") and by Save/Load's `tell_remaining` range. The tunable draw bounds satisfy `TELL_MIN_TICKS ≤ TELL_DRAW_LO ≤ TELL_DRAW_HI ≤ TELL_MAX_TICKS` (lint). **Draw bounds (CQ-4, closed): `TELL_DRAW_LO` 900 and `TELL_DRAW_HI` 1800 `[PLACEHOLDER]`**, so a tell a player meets in play lasts 15 to 30 seconds, inside Pillar 2's 10 to 30; the full 600-tick floor is reachable only through `attention_arm_tell` (AT-21) and fixtures. A second lint, `abortability_play`, binds the played range: `t_decay(ring) + REACT_ALLOW_TICKS ≤ TELL_DRAW_LO − 6` at every ring (`REACT_ALLOW_TICKS` 180 `[PLACEHOLDER]`); the R10 (b) invariant still binds at `TELL_MIN_TICKS` (AT-10). **Hard rule:** no path reaches BREACH before `tell_start_T + tell_len`, and `tell_len ≥ TELL_MIN_TICKS` on **every** path, including `attention_arm_tell` (AT-21). There is no "LOUD event → instant breach". Attention uses stream `"attention.tell"`; Horror keeps `"horror.tell"` for its own sub-tell (the kernel example is a refresh owed, C8). Rate-coupled length is a Vertical Slice playtest candidate.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-01a (written first), AC-AT-18, AC-AT-19, AC-AT-30, AC-AT-64. *Pillar.* P2. *Decided by:* R9; Pillar 2 design test; SL:528.

**AT-9. Escalation: elapsed-keyed beats and tiers.** *Rule.* Escalation is keyed to `tell_elapsed = T − tell_start_T`, **never** to progress through `tell_len`: a progress-keyed cadence peaks exactly at the breach and leaks the end, whereas keyed to elapsed time a 600-tick and an 1800-tick tell are identical for their first 600 ticks (the dread of not knowing). One SIM counter, `tell_beat`, drives **both** senses: each increment is one audio beat and one visual filament-resolve event in every lit region. Beat 0 is published in the TELL-entry run, so audio is first and the presenter may render no filament for an epoch before beat 0 exists (the art bible's rule that every tell has an audio component starting no later than its visual, HR-9). The beat interval shrinks from `BEAT_START_TICKS` (150) to `BEAT_END_TICKS` (45) over `BEAT_RAMP_TICKS` (1800), with jitter of ±`BEAT_JITTER_TICKS` (8) from a **pure integer hash** of `(tell_epoch, k, HASH_SEED)`, never from `rng_derive`, and each gap at least `BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS` (30). The schedule is built from nominal offsets, so run quantisation (a beat may publish up to five ticks late) never accumulates. `tier = 0` outside TELL and `1 + (e ≥ 600) + (e ≥ 1200)` inside, so the whole floor window is tier 1, which is Lighting's tier-1 recipe (registry `gutter_recipe`: tier 1 480/960 frames, tier 2 from `TIER2_TICKS` 300/600, tier 3 from `TIER3_TICKS` 180/360, placeholders). Filament density in a lit region is also keyed to `tell_elapsed`. **Two-channel criterion.** (auto, headless) every legal `(tell_len, seed)` publishes at least `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` (4) beats in `[tell_start_T, tell_start_T + TELL_MIN_TICKS)`; (manual, screenshot plus audio capture) the presenter renders each beat in both senses and each sense alone is sufficient (ART:954). The lamp gutter is an optional **third** channel: Lighting's tier-1 spacing (480/960 frames) can give zero dips inside the 600-tick floor window (C2), so the contract does not lean on it; the shorter tier-2/3 spacing asked for in OQ-AT-7 now exists in the registry `gutter_recipe` as placeholder bounds. Attention publishes *facts*; Adaptive Audio & Tells owns every sound, mix and cue name (its requests are in Visual/Audio Requirements and Interactions as must-repeat). **Final-gap beat suppression is dropped (CQ-5, closed):** `BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS` constrains consecutive beats only, with no beat-to-breach gap, so the worst case in the floor window is five beats (offsets 0, 158, 307, 448, 580) against `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` 4; `BEAT_START_TICKS` 135 is recorded only as the fallback if the suppression ever returns.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-20 to AC-AT-24, AC-AT-47. *Pillar.* P2. *Decided by:* R11; ART:954, :980.

**AT-10. Restraint aborts a tell.** *Rule.* During TELL, after each run's integration: if `L < theta_HOLD` then `below_hold_runs += 1`, else it resets to 0. The tell **aborts to CALM** when `below_hold_runs × 6 ≥ ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` (placeholder 120, run-quantised; the first run under the line counts as run 1), **or** at the first run with `T ≥ breach_due_T` if `L < theta_HOLD` at that run. If both are true on the same run the abort wins (one transition). An abort deposits no STRESS, switches no beacon, sets `tell_outcome = ABORTED`, freezes `tell_beat`, leaves `L` unchanged (a knob `ABORT_RELIEF_PM` defaulting to 1000, which means "no relief" and means the abort is unchanged from the ruled behaviour, lets a tuner cap the level at an abort if playtests show chained-abort fatigue) and sets `rearm_until_T = T + TELL_REARM_TICKS` (placeholder 600). A player who keeps being loud through the tell forfeits the abort; that is intended ("we chose to be loud"). Restraint usually costs the partial (Formulas F-15b), and that price is deliberate; it is balanced by a breach that has teeth (AT-11), so neither choice dominates. `tell_remaining` published to Save/Load is the time to `breach_due_T`: the conservative direction, because an abort only ends the block early. Two invariants, both lint: **(a) rearm against autosave:** `TELL_REARM_TICKS ≥ AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` (cited), which guarantees an open soft-gate window between chained aborted tells even under STRICT mode. **(b) abortability at entry:** a player who enters TELL at the highest possible level (`L_CEIL`, AT-5) and then zeroes every player-controlled input (still, beam off, no verbs) falls under `theta_HOLD` minus a margin `ATT_MARGIN` before `TELL_MIN_TICKS − 6` ticks elapse, at the ring's survivable-dark floor. In words: the tell is a window of agency even at its shortest. The play-level variant, which adds a reaction allowance and evaluates against the shortest draw a player can actually meet, is the registered formula `abortability_play` (AT-8, CQ-4; Formulas F-15b).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-25, AC-AT-26, AC-AT-27, AC-AT-16, AC-AT-52. *Pillar.* P3 (restraint is real), P2 (a tell is a window of agency). *Decided by:* R10; Pillar 3 design test.

#### Group E — The breach and its locus

**AT-11. Breach entry actions (same run).** *Rule.* At the run that satisfies `T ≥ breach_due_T` with `L ≥ theta_HOLD`: `phase = BREACH`, `breach_enter_T = T`; record `locus_occupants_at_entry` = the set of living seats whose `actor_section` is in `locus_body` at this BREACH-entry run; M17 `deposit_trace(STRESS, s, magnitude = BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT)` for every section `s` of the locus body, in ascending `section_id`, at most `STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` (placeholder 8) per run, continuing on following runs (`stress_pending_cursor`) until the body is covered; and `set_beacon(on)` submitted as a `SYSTEM` command at T+1 (CR-9 path b). The magnitude is cited by registry name and never as a number; passing that constant as `magnitude` also makes the Graph's M17 clamp reading (`stress_pm = clamp(magnitude, 1000, 3000)`) and its D.9 / LS-25 reading (`stress_pm = BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT`) agree (C15, a Graph wording refresh). **Footprint cap:** `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` bounds honoured footprints per Graph run; Attention takes at most `STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` per run. **Attention has no first claim on the cap** (CP-C5): Horror's footprints arrive by **path (a)**: the published Class B mutators `deposit_trace` (M17) and `apply_suit_damage`, called from the `horror.step` hook, which is ordered `after` Attention (KRN Interactions row 9; Horror HE-28, C-HE-3), not as SYSTEM commands in P2; Horror submits no SYSTEM command at MVP. The Graph's "first `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` in arrival order within P2" (LSG:807) carries a P2 premise that path (a) does not satisfy, so Attention claims no first place in the cap. The guarantee is therefore a **Horror must-repeat**: its per-run footprint budget is at most `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP − STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN`. The registry unit of `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` (revised 2026-10-27) says per run across all callers and subjects (global), the first 16 by arrival applied in ascending subject id and the rest dropped and counted, which matches LSG:727 (C22). A STRESS deposit the cap drops is not lost for good: the next burst run re-arms it (AT-12; an edge case below). **Attention's MVP breach is STRESS plus the beacon plus the published phase.** RUPTURE and GOUGE are Horror's; this keeps Attention fully testable without Horror. **Stance (the breach is a consequence amplifier, stated so it is a decision):** STRESS multiplies *endogenous* damage only ("the breach rhythm makes the ship *fail* faster, not *leak* faster", LSG D.9), so a breach costs the crew what they had deferred; on a clean ship it would be nearly a non-event. **Why this is not left as a stance (gate ruling, CD-GDD-ALIGN, applied):** each choice is defensible alone, but a toothless breach on a clean ship together with an abort that usually forfeits the partial (AT-10, Moment A) makes *commit* the dominant choice. Restraint would always cost the partial, riding it out would cost almost nothing, P3's "restraint and stealth are real strategies" would be empty, the "chaos" half of P2 would have no chaos, and players would learn to ignore tells. Two requirements follow, and neither is a mitigation to be traded away. **(1) Content requirement (a Derelict Authoring must-repeat).** Every body that can be a locus on MVP derelicts A and B is authored with at least one active overload or fatigue fault, so a derelict breach always has something to amplify (AC-AT-58 is the seeded-fault scenario, AC-AT-117 the authoring check). This fits P5: the outer ring is kept unsafe by wear. A body the player has repaired clean is the player's earned result and not a content failure, and a home-ship locus carries whatever the crew has left unrepaired. **(2) Horror Entities' single MVP behaviour wired to BREACH is a BLOCKING dependency** (OQ-AT-9, AC-AT-77), no longer an ask: a breach brings the thing the crew fears, not only a faster-failing hull. `BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT` is not raised to hide this. **Playtest tripwire:** the Moment-ordering guard (Player Fantasy, AC-AT-80) is named for exactly this failure: a 30-minute session with only breaches means commit has become dominant.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-28, AC-AT-29, AC-AT-30, AC-AT-58 (seeded-fault scenario). *Pillar.* P2, P3. *Decided by:* R12; LSG:196, :525, :906.

**AT-12. Breach duration and end.** *Rule.* During BREACH the level **keeps integrating** (the player's noise still matters), with `W_L[FAULT_GLOW] = W_L[BEACON] = 0` and the commanded beacon's watts excluded from both power terms (AT-4, so the alarm cannot hold the level above `theta_HOLD` and delay the exit it is tested against). STRESS is re-armed in **bursts**: `STRESS_REFRESH_BURST_RUNS` (3) consecutive runs every `STRESS_REFRESH_RUNS` (placeholder 300) runs, under the lint `STRESS_REFRESH_RUNS + STRESS_REFRESH_BURST_RUNS < STRESS_HOLD_STEPS` (cited from LSG:907; registry `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS`). A burst tolerates one dropped deposit because a deposit is idempotent. BREACH ends at the first of: (i) `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` (placeholder 3600, the death-spiral guard); (ii) after `BREACH_MIN_TICKS` (placeholder 900), `L < theta_HOLD` for `CLEAR_HOLD_TICKS` (placeholder 300, run-quantised); (iii) pod-hop departure: the last living occupant recorded at BREACH entry (`locus_occupants_at_entry`) leaving a locus body that is not the home-ship (AT-22); (iv) **ADDITION X10 (APPROVED at the CD-GDD-ALIGN gate: the combat anti-pillar explicitly permits decoys, and the lit derelict pays in STRESS on its own hardware; CR-LURE stays a playtest watch item with `LOCUS_OCC_PREF_PM` as the lever):** if at BREACH entry the locus body is not the home-ship and `locus_occupants_at_entry` is empty (it holds no living player), at `BREACH_MIN_TICKS` (the retreat the departure clause models has already happened; Moment C) (reachable at MVP only if Web Map keeps a departed derelict loaded; under LS-17's default, which Web Map adopts at WM:102, the clause covers a lit, unoccupied but loaded body). **Breach length is Attention's, not `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS`.** STRESS lapses on the Graph's own hold after the last deposit and may outlast SUBSIDING: "a long tell, a spreading wrongness, a short collapse, then silence" (LSG:986). No Attention surface shows that tail. **Must-repeat request to Adaptive Audio & Tells (R30):** `phase == CALM` is never mapped to an "all clear" cue, because the Graph's consequence sounds, not Attention, carry the ongoing wrongness; this is Audio's rule to repeat, not Attention's.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-31, AC-AT-32, AC-AT-33 (lint), AC-AT-34. *Pillar.* P2, P3. *Decided by:* R12.

**AT-13. Subsiding and the refractory.** *Rule.* On SUBSIDING entry: `set_beacon(off)` is submitted; no further STRESS deposits are made; and the level is reset to a residual, not wiped: `L ← min(L, B + POST_PM × (theta − B) / 1000)` with `POST_PM` a placeholder of 400. SUBSIDING lasts `SUBSIDE_TICKS` (placeholder 1200) while integrating normally, then the machine returns to CALM with `refract_until_T = T + REFRACT_TICKS` (placeholder 1800) and `tell_outcome = BREACHED`. During the refractory no TELL may begin, but the level keeps integrating, so **the refractory is not free safe time**: a player still loud at its end tips straight into a new tell (bounded by `L_CEIL`). The refractory is a published, state-gated valley, not a hidden scheduler. `phase` changes only inside `attention.step`; `post_restore` changes no phase and submits nothing (AT-27), so no reader can observe a half state; CALM → BREACH, TELL → SUBSIDING and BREACH → TELL do not exist. The phase table, guards and who may cause each transition are in States and Transitions.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-35, AC-AT-36, AC-AT-60 (table-driven transition test). *Pillar.* P2. *Decided by:* R13; LSG:986.

**AT-14. Locus and locus body.** *Rule.* The **locus** is the section with the highest `ledger_mAU` at TELL entry; ties go to the lowest `section_id`. It is fixed for the epoch and falls back to the home-ship's lowest `section_id` only if the locus section is cut away or goes dormant. Attribution: a PlayerId source maps to the section containing the actor's foot origin through Ship Body's `actor_section(actor_id)` (BDY-29, Section lookup for Suit; sibling in flight); an object or emitter source maps to `section_of` or the emitter's `section_id`; light and power map to their own section. The **locus body** is `sections_of(body_of(locus))` (Layout reads, LY state inventory and Body row), recorded at TELL entry as `locus_body`. STRESS covers the whole locus body: welded sections share the breach, so a welded derelict is not a safe harbour. An optional knob `LOCUS_OCC_PREF_PM` (default 1000 = "leader only", conforming) lets a tuner prefer a section holding a living player if playtests show an unattended, powered derelict absorbing most breaches. Layout's pathing asks (`room_surface`, `floor_tile_at`, `room_adjacency`, LY Interactions row for Attention / Horror) are Horror's; Attention takes only the membership reads.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-37, AC-AT-59 (decoy knob default reproduces the rule). *Pillar.* P3. *Decided by:* R14.

#### Group F — The contract, the view, the truth channel

**AT-15. The tell-window contract: one predicate for all web-originated harm.** *Rule.* `exogenous_permitted(section) = phase ∈ {BREACH, SUBSIDING} AND section ∈ locus_body`. Every web-originated exogenous call (M17 RUPTURE, GOUGE and STRESS; environmental M18 `apply_damage(..., EXOGENOUS)`) must satisfy it. A `TellAudit` ledger (a DIAGNOSTIC subscriber and a **test fixture first**, written before Horror and Audio exist) records each such call as `(tick, caller, kind, section, tell_epoch, phase)`; it also records every AT-14 fallback as `LOCUS_FALLBACK(epoch, old_body, new_body, tick)`, and the replay evaluates `section_e` against the body in force at `t_e`; the replay asserts, for every harm event `e`, that some epoch `k` satisfies `tell_start_k + tell_len_k ≤ t_e < subside_end_k` with `tell_len_k ≥ TELL_MIN_TICKS` and `section_e ∈ locus_body_k` (registry formula `tell_window_contract`). **Scope (R15, provisional):** TellAudit also records every lethal act by a horror on a crew body, because the Pillar 2 promise ("every breach is preceded by a 10–30 second tell") is a promise to the *player*, not to the hull; a lethal act outside a told breach is exactly the ambush Pillar 2 forbids, and Horror's stalk is additive, never a substitute. **Horror Entities must REPEAT this rule or surface a CONFLICT.** "Perceivable" is defined relative to **every living player**, not to the locus: the tell must be audible to all of them through the non-positional structure-borne bed, whose existence and listener scope are contractual (its dB margin is Audio's). The bed is what makes a tell on an unoccupied lit derelict fair. Player-originated exogenous acts (cut, vent) never need the tell (LSG:144: agency); the Graph's endogenous gate (`W_MIN_WARNING_S`) is not how this contract is enforced and neither replaces nor is replaced by it. **This rule discharges LSG:1063** ("every call site of M17 RUPTURE, GOUGE and STRESS and every environmental M18 `apply_damage(…, EXOGENOUS)` source is preceded by that system's own 10–30 s Pillar 2 tell, named in its GDD, and none relies on the Graph's gate for it").
*Criterion.* AC-AT-01 (first; discharges LSG:1063), AC-AT-01a, AC-AT-68 (the provisional crew-body scope). *Pillar.* P2. *Decided by:* R15; LSG:144, :1063.

**AT-16. The published read view.** *Rule.* `attention.view` is a SIM read view of integers, never `PERCEPTION_OUT` (AC-SL-95a, AC-SL-121). The field set is final (Save/Load and Audio restate these names):

| Field | Values | Reader and note |
|---|---|---|
| `phase` | CALM, TELL, BREACH, SUBSIDING | Audio, Lighting, Presenter, Sanity, Horror. **Audio reads `phase`, never `breach_phase`** (lint) |
| `breach_phase` | CALM, BREACH, SUBSIDING | **Save/Load only.** TELL is reported as CALM so Save/Load's FAIR mode stays live (C5); not an edit to Save/Load |
| `tell_active` | 0/1 | Save/Load |
| `tell_remaining` | 0 to `TELL_MAX_TICKS`, 0 outside TELL | Save/Load, SIM readers (Horror) and the DIAGNOSTIC Debug Overlay only; **never a PRESENTATION subscriber** |
| `tell_elapsed` | 0 to `TELL_MAX_TICKS`, 0 outside TELL | Audio, Presenter |
| `tell_beat`, `tell_beat_T` | counter, tick | Audio, Presenter |
| `tier` | 0 to 3 | Lighting (`tier ≥ 1`), Audio, Sanity |
| `tell_outcome` | NONE, ABORTED, BREACHED | Audio, Sanity; NONE until the first tell ends, ABORTED from an abort until the next TELL entry, BREACHED from SUBSIDING end until the next TELL entry; survives load |
| `tell_epoch` | int, +1 at every TELL entry | all (replaces the brief's `breach_epoch`, because aborted tells have no breach) |
| `phase_enter_T`, `breach_enter_T` | ticks | Audio, Presenter |
| `level_pm` | 0 to 1000, baseline-relative: `min(1000, ⌊1000 × (L − B) / (theta − B)⌋)` | Presenter and Audio, **quantised only** (AT-26) |
| `locus_section_id` | id | Audio, Horror |
| `exogenous_permitted(section)` | predicate | Horror, Graph-side callers |

`tell_progress_pm` is **not published** (it would leak the end). Audio's dedupe key is the pair `(tell_epoch, tell_beat)` compared lexicographically, because a new tell resets `tell_beat` to 0 while `tell_epoch` increases. Published-field table by phase is in States and Transitions. The Save/Load gate formula (`attention_tell_block`, `soft_block`) is owned by Save/Load; Attention owns only the three field *values* and their names. **The read view of Save/Load's SL-5 Soft (b) (answers Save/Load OQ-24):** the condition reads `attention.view` (SIM), and nothing else of Attention: the active tell is `tell_active` = 1, its remaining ticks are `tell_remaining` (compared by Save/Load against `W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE` under `gate_tell_mode` FAIR; STRICT defers for the whole tell), and the breach phase is `breach_phase` (CALM, BREACH, SUBSIDING; TELL reports CALM, C5). **Pod-hop notification:** the name is Web Map & Transit's, not Attention's; Web Map WM-31 names `POD_DEPARTED` and `POD_ARRIVED` (names provisional until its registry merge), and `POD_ARRIVED` is the one it lists Save/Load as consuming as an event trigger. Attention needs neither for correctness (AT-22 derives departure from Ship Body).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-38, AC-AT-39, AC-AT-40, AC-AT-66, AC-AT-67, AC-SL-121 (discharged). *Pillar.* P1, P2. *Decided by:* R16; SL:517, :528, :531.

**AT-17. The tell is a truth channel.** *Rule.* Nothing Attention publishes or presents is removed, added, delayed or altered by the Perception Filter or by Sanity. There is **no phantom tell and no false calm, in any tier and at any stage** (gate ruling, CD-GDD-ALIGN: rejected by pillar, permanent, not deferred): a false calm hides a real tell and produces exactly the ambush Pillar 2 forbids, and a phantom tell spends the player's restraint on a lie and voids `TellAudit`. The tell is a standing truth channel in every tier; Sanity keeps its own disjoint sound family and its own rim fringe (AV-17). The art bible's tell element "a needle that drifts on a *real* gauge" (ART 2.7) is **dropped from the tell (C1)**: no Graph or instrument value may take Attention as input (LSG:281), and a gauge moving with no physical cause would be a lie that Failure & Trail refuses to draw (FT-4); a real needle change caused by breach STRESS damage is a breach *consequence*, not a tell, and the wording amendment is routed to the art-director and In-World Instruments. Attention reads no sanity and no `PERCEPTION_OUT` value (CR-12).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-41, AC-AT-40. *Pillar.* P4, P1. *Decided by:* R17.

#### Group G — The world writes and the presenter

**AT-18. The beacon.** *Rule.* Attention **concurs** that BEACON `gates_actions` is **false** (the registry `emitter_kind` row is marked "provisional, user decision pending" and EM:200 routes the decision to the technical-director and the game-designer, so Attention does not make it; the beacon sweeps the cone over a point for roughly a tenth of each sweep, which is the reason Attention concurs). The Emitter State's `set_beacon` `max_age_ticks` of **120 is confirmed by Attention** (EM asks for exactly that): a T+1 SYSTEM command with 20 runs of slack. **Which beacon:** per Derelict Authoring DA-24 *(sibling in flight, `derelict-authoring-handmade-kits.md`, status In Design)*, exactly one BEACON exists in the world, on `home_ship` in `spine_corridor`, authored with `node_ref` = `bcn_main`, a dedicated LOAD node (`mod_lamp`, 20 W, DA-24; OQ-DA-9 closed 'no sharing') under the lint `BEACON_COUNT` (ERROR, BOOT); derelicts A and B have none. DA-24 adds "Attention … may relocate it": **placement is Authoring's data row and is not Attention's** (refresh owed to DA, C20); Attention only submits `set_beacon` to the one beacon the data holds. Attention switches that beacon on at **every** BREACH entry regardless of locus, because it asserts a true global fact ("a breach is on"); the positional creak layer (AUD-4 a) and STRESS carry *where*; the non-positional bed carries *that*. It switches off at SUBSIDING entry. `W_L[BEACON] = 0`. **The beacon is also a Graph LOAD** (its own LOAD node `bcn_main`, 20 W, DA-24), so switching it on adds draw and a `delta_w_last5` step on the home-ship. In BREACH and SUBSIDING Attention therefore **excludes the commanded beacon's watts from both power terms** (AT-4, `W_P` and `W_S`), so the alarm cannot feed the level that the BREACH exit tests (CP-C3; the open sub-question for the Graph and Emitter owners is in the C11 ask). `fx_cryo_lamp` (DA-23) is the one `node_ref` 0 fixture and is never touched by the tell. Under reduced flashing the beacon becomes a static mark (LO-26). LO-26 also names a HUD glyph, which contradicts ART:741 and is routed as C27, so the static mark and the audio carry the breach. A rejected `set_beacon` (`NOT_LIVE` when home-ship power is dead, `NO_SUCH_EMITTER`, `NOT_BEACON`) is logged once and retried once per run for `BEACON_RETRY_RUNS` (placeholder 20, equal to the command's max age); it never blocks the machine. Alarm cadence (one pulse per `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS`) is a must-repeat request to Adaptive Audio, not an Attention rule.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-42, AC-AT-29, AC-AT-63 (beacon exclusion from the power terms). *Pillar.* P2. *Decided by:* R18; EM:165, :200.

**AT-19. The suppressant: contract only.** *Rule.* The contract is stated and **no effect verb ships at MVP**: Attention reads `tool_charge(player_id, def_id)` (0..`charges_max`; `def_id` = `tool_suppressant`) through an ordered read and is never pushed to by Suit; the verb's registrant calls `consume_charge(player_id, def_id) -> ok`; and `OUT_OF_CHARGES` is the repeated read name (SE-14 and its open question 7, ruled CK-5). The effect is a Vertical Slice addendum. It is worded "repel or distract", never "fight" or "kill", and it may not bypass restraint (a suppressant that aborts a tell outright would make restraint an item). The index edge Attention → Suit is downgraded to "soft, none at MVP" (refresh owed, C14). Answer to IDB OQ-16: at MVP the suppressant has no Attention effect, full or empty; any MVP effect is Horror Entities' (repel or distract, never touching the level or a tell). Horror ships none (HE-39), so the slot-4 tool would be inert at MVP. **Reconciled with Horror C-HE-6:** the creative-director position, confirmed at Horror's CD-GDD-ALIGN gate as BLOCKING (HE-39, AC-HE-124), removes `tool_suppressant` from the MVP start kit and MVP item list (rows owed by Horror to Suit & Equipment and the Item & Module Database); Attention agrees, because an inert tool is a Pillar 1 lie. The contract names above stand for the Vertical Slice addendum, and Attention declares `after: suit.vitals` only if a future revision reads Suit state (none at MVP).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-43. *Pillar.* P3. *Decided by:* R19; IA:133, SE:201, IDB:249.

**AT-20. The Attention Presenter (the world tells).** *Rule.* A PRESENTATION subscriber owned by this GDD draws, at MVP: (a) rim **filaments only inside a lit region of a player-controllable real emitter** (the gate widening, below; unlit space draws none: Principle 1, ART:302), and (b) a **dust-direction change in the beam**. **Hull-stress hairlines are cut from MVP** and routed to the art-director: they read too easily as the Integrity trail (ART:312, :565) and risk Pillar 1. Filaments are web pieces in the Y-sort layer (rotation permitted for web and horror pieces, PIPE row 16); colour is the web's iridescence only (teal → green → bronze → gold, ART:1371), never flesh or violet. All presentation randomness is a pure integer hash, never `rng_derive` (the Lighting precedent, LO-19). The camera does nothing (PIPE:151); the beam, ambient light, camera and controls do not change in any phase (ART 2.7, PCI:135). **"Lit region" (gate ruling CD-GDD-ALIGN, OQ-AT-3 closed).** A strand resolves at the edge of the lit region of a **player-controllable real emitter**: the eligible kinds are exactly `BEAM` (the cone), `FIXTURE`, `NODE_LANTERN` and `DEPLOYABLE` (a pool, with a rim band of `RIM_BAND_PM` of the emitter's reach), the kinds of the registry's closed `emitter_kind` list that the player can switch, power, carry or place. **Excluded:** `BEACON` (the alarm lens is not a tell carrier), the cryo EMERGENCY lamp `fx_cryo_lamp` (FIXTURE subkind EMERGENCY, self-powered; keeps LO-20, "never a tell carrier") and `FAULT_GLOW` (a latent fault's glow is a consequence, not a tell). Why: with cone-only, a player whose chest lamp is off while their own fixtures burn would get no visual channel for a tell their own fixtures caused, which contradicts P3's lesson that the web sees the ship, not the body (Moment C), and P2's "a careful player could have perceived" for a deaf player. **Principle 1 is unchanged: a strand is never drawn in unlit space**, a site lit only by an excluded emitter is not eligible, and strands appear only in the **true** lit region, never in a falsely extended reach. **Producer scope fallback:** cone-only (BEAM regions alone) remains allowed if the producer must cut scope for MVP; it is recorded as scope and not as creative direction, and the deaf-player channel then reduces to the beam. In co-op, filaments resolve in every lit region, each beat shared (SHARED_SUMMARY).
*Criterion.* AC-AT-44, AC-AT-45, AC-AT-53. *Pillar.* P3, P1. *Decided by:* R20; PIPE:305.

#### Group H — Seams, determinism, scope

**AT-21. The arming seam (test and onboarding).** *Rule.* `attention_arm_tell {tell_len}` has `allowed_origins {SYSTEM}`, no player binding and no PLAYER origin. It is valid only in CALM (otherwise rejected `NOT_CALM`); `tell_len` is clamped to `[TELL_MIN_TICKS, TELL_MAX_TICKS]`; the tell then follows every rule of AT-8 to AT-13. It serves test fixtures and the Vertical Slice's hand-led onboarding script and is listed in the control manifest as non-gameplay. It is not a cheat: the floor still binds.
*Criterion.* AC-AT-19. *Pillar.* P2. *Decided by:* R21.

**AT-22. Dormancy and pod hop *(provisional — Web Map & Transit undesigned)*.** *Rule.* Dormant sections give no stimuli and hold no ledger. `IN_POD` holds the departure ring (AT-6); a hop between two ring-1 loci (the MVP's only kind, HOME and a ring-1 derelict) holds `held_ring` 1 throughout, so `B` does not change (Web Map ASK-A1). The pod's running light is cosmetic presentation, not authored as an emitter (WM-26): it is not an eligible tell region (AT-20) and not a light stimulus (AT-4). A pod hop does **not** interrupt a TELL.
