**What each dependent must repeat in its own GDD** (so the dependency is bidirectional, per the design-docs rule): the **Controller** pushes a class and repeats Body's timing text; **Interaction** repeats IR-1 to IR-3 as accepted; the **Graph** repeats that Attention is the caller of M17 STRESS at the breach-rhythm transition (any Horror STRESS lands inside the same `exogenous_permitted` predicate) and defines `power_use`'s fields; the **Emitter State** repeats the confirmed `max_age_ticks` and records Attention's concurrence on `gates_actions` (the decision stays with its routed owners); **Lighting** repeats the four must-repeats above; **Save/Load** repeats the exact view names; **Horror**, **Audio**, **Sanity**, **Web Map** and **Assembly** repeat the rows above.

## Formulas

*Conventions (read first; every formula below uses them).* **Units.** The level is stored in **mAU** (64-bit integer); 1 AU = 1000 mAU = the noise of one walking tick (the Controller-owned registry row `NOISE_PER_TICK`, cited; its walk value defines the unit). `theta = ATT_THRESHOLD × 1000` mAU; `theta_HOLD = theta × HOLD_PM / 1000`; `L_CEIL = theta × L_CEIL_PM / 1000`; `TAU_ATT_TICKS` is written `tau` where space is short. All durations are ticks at `TICK_RATE`; a *run* is one `attention.step` execution at `T` a multiple of 6. **Integer rule (kernel determinism).** Everything that is hashed, persisted or replicated is integer: `floor()` is applied to non-negative operands only, `ceil(a / b)` for `a ≥ 0` is written `(a + b − 1) div b`, no float is used in the sim, and a float appears only in the closed-form *oracles* of the tests. **Weights** `W_L`, `W_P`, `W_S` are in micro-AU per unit per tick (1000 = 1 mAU per unit per tick), so `× 6 / 1000` turns a per-tick weight into mAU per run in one dimension (CQ-7: the systems-designer's single dimension; the game-designer's felt-target table is rationale and the playtest alternative, F-25). **Placeholders used in the worked examples** (all `[PLACEHOLDER]`, tuned in `assets/data/attention/`): `ATT_THRESHOLD` 6000 AU (`theta` 6,000,000), `HOLD_PM` 800 (`theta_HOLD` 4,800,000), `L_CEIL_PM` 1020 (`L_CEIL` 6,120,000), `TAU_ATT_TICKS` 1800, `ATT_MARGIN` 50 AU, `B_table` {ring 1: 300, ring 2: 450, ring 3: 550} AU, `U_TOOL` 4, `U_LOUD` 180, `W_L` 1000 for every lit kind, `W_P` 100, `W_S` 6000, `DARK_FLOOR_LOAD_W` 1500 W. **Cited, never re-derived:** the per-emitter term of EM D.12 (`section_emission`), KRN D.10 (`rng_derive`, `rng_int`), `ticks_for_seconds`, Graph D.11 (`fairness_gate`, `W_MIN_STEPS`), Save/Load's `autosave_gate`. **Evaluation order inside one run (the formulas depend on it):** (1) drain `pending_mAU` into `I_noise` (F-1); (2) read `emitters()` and `power_use` (F-3, F-4); (3) integrate `L` (F-5); (4) update the ledger (F-6); (5) evaluate the phase logic on the integrated `L` (F-9, F-14, F-17); (6) perform entry and exit actions (M17 deposits, `set_beacon`, F-18, F-19); (7) compute the beat schedule, tier and the published view (F-7, F-12, F-13). A TELL-entry run therefore publishes beat 0 (step 7) and runs no abort check on that same run.

#### F-1. Noise intake (pending accumulator)

The noise_intake formula is defined as:

`pending_mAU[src] = min(L_CEIL, pending_mAU[src] + U[class] × 1000)` per accepted push; `I_noise = Σ_src pending_mAU[src]` at step (1); then `pending_mAU[src] = 0` for all `src`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `U[class]` | int AU | 0 to `U_LOUD` (knob range up to 400) | the intake table (AT-3): `QUIET` 0; `WALK`, `RUN`, `CARRY` cited from `NOISE_PER_TICK` (walk 1 / run 4 / carry 1); `TOOL` = `U_TOOL`; `LOUD` = `U_LOUD` | attention units added by one push |
| `src` | PlayerId or object ref | one entry per distinct source | `push_stimulus(kind, source_ref, class)` | the attribution key; resolved to a section at integration (F-6) |
| `pending_mAU[src]` | int64 mAU | 0 to `L_CEIL` | block `attention.state` (persisted) | noise accumulated since the last run; saturates at `L_CEIL` |
| `I_noise` | int64 mAU | 0 to (sources × `L_CEIL`) | derived | the noise input of this run |

**Output Range:** one source, one run: walking 6,000 mAU; running or carrying-while-running 24,000 mAU (PCI:560: 0 to 24 per run); a `TOOL` channel run 24,000 mAU; one `LOUD` push 180,000 mAU. The saturation at `L_CEIL` changes nothing below the ceiling (the level is clamped there anyway, F-5) and gives the overflow edge case a defined result (*orchestrator ruling: the saturation is added so an unbounded stack of pushes in one run has a bounded 64-bit result*). An unknown class adds 0 and increments a counter.
**Example:** the player runs for 6 ticks and a cut completes in the same run: `24,000 + 180,000 = 204,000` mAU. A 180-tick patch (`TOOL` pushed once per accrued tick) pushes `180 × 4 × 1000 = 720,000` mAU = 720 AU (IA D.9: equal to running for the same 180 ticks, four times walking).

#### F-2. Source-to-section attribution (feeds the ledger, not the level)

The source_attribution formula is defined as:

`section(src) = actor_section(src)` for a PlayerId source (foot-origin lookup); `section_of(src)` for an object source; `emitter.section_id` for light; the `power_use` section for power; a push whose section is dormant or unknown is dropped and counted

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `actor_section(actor_id)` | section id | loaded sections | Ship Body read *(provisional — Body is a sibling in flight)* | the section holding the actor's foot origin |
| `section_of(ref)` | section id | loaded sections | Layout / owning system | the section of an object source |
| `dropped_unattributed` | int counter | 0 to unbounded | DIAGNOSTIC | pushes dropped because the section was dormant or unknown |

**Output Range:** a loaded section id or a counted drop. A drop removes the push from the **ledger only**; the push still counts toward `I_noise` and `L` (the web hears a sound whose source it cannot place; the level is the world's, the locus is a choice). **Example:** the player in derelict A (`sec_a_2`) pushes `RUN` for 6 ticks: 24,000 mAU enter `I_noise` and 24,000 mAU enter `ledger[sec_a_2]` (F-6).

#### F-3. Light stimulus

The light_stimulus formula is defined as:

`I_light[s] = ( Σ_{e in emitters(s)} W_L[kind_e] × floor(output_pm_e × reach_px_e / 1000) × 6 ) div 1000`, and `I_light = Σ_s I_light[s]`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `W_L[kind]` | int (micro-AU per unit per tick) | 0 to 4000; 1000 for `BEAM`, `FIXTURE`, `NODE_LANTERN`, `DEPLOYABLE`; `FAULT_GLOW` 1000 in CALM and TELL and **0** in BREACH and SUBSIDING; `BEACON` **0** always (lint) | `assets/data/attention/weights` | the per-kind weight |
| `output_pm_e` | int | 0 to 1000 | emitter record (`emitters()`) | the emitter's steady output |
| `reach_px_e` | int px | 8 to 384 (EM shape table) | emitter record | the emitter's current reach |
| `floor(output_pm_e × reach_px_e / 1000)` | int | 0 to 384 | **cited: EM D.12 per-emitter term** | the Emitter State's own term, never re-derived |
| `kind_e` | enum | the closed `emitter_kind` list | emitter record | selects the weight |
| `emitters(s)` | list | at most `MAX_EMITTERS` in the world | Emitter State | every emitter of loaded section `s`, lit or not as the record reports `output_pm` |
| `I_light` | int64 mAU | 0 to 884,736 per run at the weight cap | derived | light input of this run |

**Output Range:** 0 with no lit emitter. At the placeholder weights the maximum is `96 × 384 × 1000 × 6 div 1000 = 221,184` mAU per run (221 AU, `MAX_EMITTERS` emitters all at output 1000 and reach 384); 884,736 at the weight cap 4000. The owner's beam is counted **once**, from the emitter list at apex value; `lux_pm` is never read as a stimulus and `probe()` is never called (EM-21, EM-27). `served_pm` is not a stimulus: it reaches Attention as light through `output_pm`.
**Example:** the owner's wide beam (output 1000, reach 288, term 288), three home fixtures (1000, 144) and the cryo EMERGENCY lamp (1000, 144) all in one section: `(1000 × (288 + 4 × 144) × 6) div 1000 = (1000 × 864 × 6) div 1000 = 5,184` mAU per run. A live glowstick (1000, 96) adds `576` mAU per run; the stack cap `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` (four) adds 2,304: light is a spend, never a safe state.
*Derivation of `W_L[BEAM]` = 1000:* the wide beam's term 288 at weight 1000 is 288 mAU per tick, about 0.29 of a walking tick; running plus the beam from `L = B` settles toward `B + 4,288 × 1800 mAU` and crosses `theta` in 2,412 ticks (40 s) in the integer model (R4's calibration "about 45 s" is the same order); walking alone settles `1 AU × tau` = 1,800 AU above `B`, 30 percent of `theta`.

#### F-4. Power stimulus

The power_stimulus formula is defined as:

`I_power[s] = ( W_P × max(0, draw_w − lamps_lit_w − beacon_w) × 6 ) div 1000 + ( W_S × max(0, delta_w_last5 − beacon_step_w) × 6 ) div 1000`, with `beacon_w = beacon_step_w = 0` outside BREACH and SUBSIDING, and `W_N × noisy_w` reserved at `W_N` = 0

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `W_P` | int (micro-AU per W per tick) | 0 to 300; placeholder 100 | data | steady non-lamp draw weight ("surplus draw is never free", LSG:794) |
| `W_S` | int (micro-AU per W per tick) | 0 to 20000; placeholder 6000 | data | transient (inrush) weight (the hiding verb's cost, LSG:809) |
| `draw_w` | int W | 0 to 10,000 per section | `power_use(section_id)` *(assumed semantics, C11)* | the section's total served load this step |
| `lamps_lit_w` | int W | 0 to `draw_w` | `power_use` | the lamp subset of `draw_w`, subtracted so a lamp is not counted twice (EM-27) |
| `delta_w_last5` | int W (signed) | −10,000 to 10,000 | `power_use` *(assumed: `draw_w` now minus `draw_w` five steps ago; may include lamp watts, C11)* | the transient the inrush produces |
| `beacon_w` | int W | 0 to the beacon's declared `req_w` | the BEACON record's load, read while `phase` is BREACH or SUBSIDING *(provisional — value is an Emitter/Authoring data row, DA-24)* | the commanded beacon's own draw; 0 outside BREACH and SUBSIDING (CP-C3) |
| `beacon_step_w` | int W | 0 to `beacon_w` | the beacon's load step inside the five-step window of its switch-on, BREACH and SUBSIDING only | the beacon's contribution to `delta_w_last5`; 0 otherwise (CP-C3) |
| `noisy_w` | int W | reserved | `power_use` | no definition exists anywhere (C11); weight `W_N` 0, flagged |

**Output Range:** 0 for an idle or absent section; a dead (SCRAM) section reports no draw and gives 0. The cold start of the whole tier 0 and 1 branch at `INRUSH_PM` 1500 (the Graph's data, default 1500 permille over `INRUSH_STEPS` 5 steps) takes draw from 0 to 1,500 W and gives `(6000 × 1500 × 6) div 1000 = 54,000` mAU for each of five runs = 270 AU in total (4.5 percent of `theta`). If `power_use` is absent for a section, `I_power[s]` is 0 and one diagnostic is logged per process.
**Example (steady):** home-ship section, `draw_w` 2,500, `lamps_lit_w` 600, no beacon: `(100 × 1900 × 6) div 1000 = 1,140` mAU per run. **Example (beacon exclusion):** BREACH, `draw_w` 2,520 (including the 20 W beacon lamp `bcn_main`, DA-24), `lamps_lit_w` 600, `delta_w_last5` +20 (the beacon's switch-on), `beacon_w` 20, `beacon_step_w` 20: `(100 × (2520 − 600 − 20) × 6) div 1000 + (6000 × max(0, 20 − 20) × 6) div 1000 = 1,140 + 0 = 1,140`; without the exclusion the same run would add `732` mAU (12 steady plus 720 transient) for five runs, `3,660` mAU in all, about 3.7 AU of alarm feeding the level that the BREACH exit tests. *C11 evidence:* LSG Example C states the inrush "1200 W spike in `power_use.delta_w_last5`" where the 1200 W includes 300 W of lamps, so `delta_w_last5` appears to include lamp watts and a lamp switch-on would be a bounded five-run double count (`5 × floor(6000 × 1200 × 6 / 1000) = 216` AU of which the lamp share, 300 of 1200, is 54 AU). The ask to the Graph owner defines each field; if `delta_w_last5` does include lamp watts and the Graph cannot exclude them, Attention subtracts `delta(lamps_lit_w)` itself.
*Choice of `W_P` 100:* the MVP home-ship tier 0 and 1 load is 1,000 W (LSG:433), giving 100 mAU per tick = 180 AU at steady state, small enough to leave the survivable-dark floor (F-16) room. The ceiling is `W_P` 116 at ring 3 with the two wake glows (each +1 adds 2.7 AU to `x_dark` at 1,500 W and the ring-3 slack is 12 AU in level terms).

#### F-5. The integrator (registry: `attention_integrator`)

The attention_integrator formula is defined as:

`dec = max(0, (6 × (L − B) + tau − 1) div tau)`; `L' = min(L_CEIL, max(B, L + I_run − dec))`; `I_run = I_noise + I_light + I_power`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L` | int64 mAU | `B` to `L_CEIL` | block (persisted) | the level before this run |
| `B` | int64 mAU | 300,000 to about 724,000 at the placeholder rows (about 594,000 with the two wake glows) | F-8 | the baseline for the current ring |
| `tau` | int ticks | 300 to 3600 (at least 300 by IR-3), placeholder 1800 | `TAU_ATT_TICKS` | the single memory constant |
| `I_run` | int64 mAU | at least 0 | F-1, F-3, F-4 | this run's input |
| `L_CEIL` | int64 mAU | `theta` to 1.1 `theta` | data (`L_CEIL_PM`, ADDITION X1 adopted by checkpoint ruling CQ-2 and approved at the CD-GDD-ALIGN gate) | the saturation level |
| `dec` | int64 mAU | 0 to `(L_CEIL − B) × 6 / 1800 + 1` | derived | the ceiling decay of this run, computed on the **pre-input** `L` |

**Output Range:** `[B, L_CEIL]` = [300,000, 6,120,000] mAU at ring 1. `dec` is at least 1 whenever `L > B`, so with zero input the level reaches `B` **exactly** (the integer truncation stall of LSG:464 cannot occur); with large input it saturates at `L_CEIL`. Under a constant input `r` (mAU per tick) the steady level is `L_ss = B + r × tau` (the per-run fixed point has `dec = 6r`). If a ring change raises `B` above `L`, then `L − B` is negative, `dec` is 0 and `L` is lifted to `B` in the same run; if `B` falls, `L` decays toward the new floor.
**Example:** `B` 300,000; `L` 2,000,000; this run: running 24,000, beam 1,728, three home fixtures 2,592, home power 1,140, EMERGENCY lamp 864, so `I_run` = 30,324; `dec = (6 × 1,700,000 + 1799) div 1800 = 5,667`; `L' = 2,000,000 + 30,324 − 5,667 = 2,024,657`. *Stance:* the level is a **rate detector with a 30-second memory**, not a session score (AT-5); a TELL means "loud or lit at a high rate for roughly half a minute to a minute", and `tau` is capped from above by abortability (F-15).

#### F-6. Section ledger and the locus (locus choice only)

The section_ledger formula is defined as:

`ledger'[s] = min(L_CEIL, max(0, ledger[s] + I_s − (6 × ledger[s] + tau − 1) div tau))`; `score[s] = ledger[s] × (1000 if occupied(s) else LOCUS_OCC_PREF_PM) div 1000`; `locus = argmax_s score[s]`, ties to the lowest `section_id`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ledger[s]` | int64 mAU | 0 to `L_CEIL` | block (persisted, sorted by `section_id`) | the per-section memory, same `tau` as `L` |
| `I_s` | int64 mAU | at least 0 | F-1 to F-4 attributed by F-2 | the part of `I_run` attributed to section `s` |
| `occupied(s)` | bool | | the Controller's `loco_state` and `actor_section` *(provisional on Body)* | whether a living player is in `s` |
| `LOCUS_OCC_PREF_PM` | int permille | 600 to 1000; default 1000 | data | 1000 means "leader only" (conforming to R14); lower values discount unoccupied sections |

**Output Range:** `ledger[s]` in `[0, L_CEIL]`; a dormant section holds no entry; the locus is evaluated once, at TELL entry, and fixed for the epoch (AT-14). **Example:** derelict A's ledger 3,000,000 with `I_s` 30,324: decay `(6 × 3,000,000 + 1799) div 1800 = 10,000`, so `ledger' = 3,020,324`; the lit, idle home ship has a steady ledger of about 1,379,000 (`766 × 1800`); locus = derelict A. If the player left A's fixtures lit and now welds loudly at home, the home ledger can win: the intended P3 consequence. A tie at 2,000,000 each goes to the lower `section_id`.

#### F-7. Published level (`level_pm`)

The level_publication formula is defined as:

`level_pm = min(1000, (1000 × (L − B)) div (theta − B))`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L`, `B`, `theta` | int64 mAU | as F-5 | block, F-8, data | level, baseline and threshold |
| `level_pm` | int | 0 to 1000 | derived | baseline-relative, **never drawn directly** |

**Output Range:** 0 at `L = B`; 1000 at `L ≥ theta`. Lint: `theta > B` at every ring (a division by zero otherwise). Presentation may use `level_pm` only after the quantisation of F-24; no continuous mapping exists (AT-26). **Example:** `L` 3,000,000, `B` 300,000: `(1000 × 2,700,000) div 5,700,000 = 473`.

#### F-8. Baseline by depth

The baseline_by_depth formula is defined as:

`B = B_table[ring_B] × 1000`, with `ring_B = max over sections s containing a living player of ring_of(s)`, `held_ring` while a seat is IN_POD

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `B_table[ring]` | int AU | non-decreasing in `ring`; placeholder 300 / 450 / 550 | `assets/data/attention/baseline` (rows beyond ring 1 are test rows; Depth Rings owns them in the Vertical Slice) | the baseline per ring |
| `ring_of(s)` | int | 1 and up | the registry formula `ring_of` (Web Map & Transit): the owning record's ring (welded to HOME, parked pod to `pod_at`'s record, mid-leg pod to the departure record); absent or sub-1 resolves to 1, logged once per process | the section's ring |
| `held_ring` | int | 1 and up | block (persisted) | the departure ring held while a seat is `IN_POD` |

**Output Range:** 300,000 to 550,000 mAU at the placeholder rows; the hard ceiling is the F-15 cap (about 724 AU at the clean-ship dark load and about 594 AU with the two wake glows, F-15, F-16). Invariants (lint): `B_table` non-decreasing; F-16 holds at every row with `N_GLOW_DARK` = the glows authored active at wake. **Example:** a player in derelict A (ring 1) and a crewmate at home (ring 1): `B = 300,000`; the player pods to a ring 2 section: the ring of the departure section is held until arrival, then `B = 450,000` and `L` is lifted to `B` if lower.

#### F-9. TELL entry guard

The tell_entry formula is defined as:

`enter_TELL = (phase == CALM) AND (L' ≥ theta) AND (T ≥ refract_until_T) AND (T ≥ rearm_until_T)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L'` | int64 mAU | `B` to `L_CEIL` | F-5 | the level after this run's integration |
| `refract_until_T` | int ticks | 0 and up | block | set at SUBSIDING end |
| `rearm_until_T` | int ticks | 0 and up | block | set at an abort |

**Output Range:** boolean; the guards are inclusive, so entry exactly at a timer boundary is legal. No catch-up elapsed time is credited when the level sat above `theta` during a timer. `attention_arm_tell` replaces the whole guard except `phase == CALM` (F-10): it is a SYSTEM-origin test and onboarding seam that does not honour the two timers, the floor still binds, and a fixture or script arms only from a clean state (*orchestrator ruling*). **Example:** `L'` 6,000,000 at `T` 7,200 with `refract_until_T` 5,400 and `rearm_until_T` 6,000: true.

#### F-10. Tell draw

The tell_draw formula is defined as:

`tell_len = rng_int(TELL_DRAW_LO, TELL_DRAW_HI)` using `rng_derive(world_seed, "attention.tell", rng_counter)`, then `rng_counter += 1`; armed: `tell_len = clamp(arg, TELL_MIN_TICKS, TELL_MAX_TICKS)` with no draw

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TELL_DRAW_LO`, `TELL_DRAW_HI` | int ticks | `TELL_MIN_TICKS` to `TELL_MAX_TICKS`; placeholders **900** and 1800 (CQ-4) | data | the played range, 15 to 30 s |
| `TELL_MIN_TICKS` | int ticks | 600 | **derived**: `ticks_for_seconds(W_MIN_WARNING_S)` | the floor; never a knob |
| `TELL_MAX_TICKS` | int ticks | 1800 | fixed by P2's "10 to 30 s" and SL:528 | the cap |
| `rng_counter` | int | 0 and up | block (persisted) | the stream position; restore cannot reroll |
| `arg` | int ticks | any | `attention_arm_tell {tell_len}` (SYSTEM origin) | the armed length, clamped |

**Output Range:** 900 to 1800 ticks for an organic tell (mean 1350); 600 to 1800 only through `attention_arm_tell`. Lint: `TELL_MIN_TICKS ≤ TELL_DRAW_LO ≤ TELL_DRAW_HI ≤ TELL_MAX_TICKS`. *(Orchestrator ruling: an armed tell does not consume `rng_counter`, so arming never shifts the stream of later organic tells.)* **Example:** counter 3 with the stub stream yielding 1,044 gives `tell_len` 1,044 (inside 900 to 1800); `attention_arm_tell {tell_len: 100}` is clamped to 600 and leaves `rng_counter` unchanged.

#### F-11. Remaining and due

The tell_remaining formula is defined as:

`breach_due_T = tell_start_T + tell_len`; `tell_remaining(T) = max(0, breach_due_T − T)` in TELL, else 0; the BREACH-or-abort decision happens at the first run with `T ≥ breach_due_T`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tell_start_T`, `tell_len` | int ticks | as F-10 | block | the seeded, fixed window |
| `breach_due_T` | int ticks | | block | the earliest breach tick |
| `tell_remaining` | int ticks | 0 to `TELL_MAX_TICKS` | published to Save/Load, SIM readers and the DIAGNOSTIC Debug Overlay only | the conservative time to `breach_due_T` |

**Output Range:** 0 to 1800, strictly non-increasing within an epoch. The BREACH decision falls in `[breach_due_T, breach_due_T + 5]` and **never earlier** (AC-AT-30). **Example:** `tell_start_T` 7,200 and `tell_len` 1,044: at `T` 7,800 `tell_remaining` = 444; the due run is `T` 8,244 (a multiple of 6, so no lateness). With `tell_len` 1,047 the due run is 8,250 (3 ticks late).

#### F-12. Beat schedule (elapsed-keyed)

The beat_schedule formula is defined as:

`off[0] = 0`; `off[k+1] = max(off[k] + BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS, off[k] + interval(off[k]) + jit(k))`; `interval(e) = BEAT_START_TICKS − ((BEAT_START_TICKS − BEAT_END_TICKS) × min(e, BEAT_RAMP_TICKS)) div BEAT_RAMP_TICKS`; `jit(k) = (hash32(tell_epoch, k, HASH_SEED) mod (2 × BEAT_JITTER_TICKS + 1)) − BEAT_JITTER_TICKS`; beat `k` is published on the first run with `T ≥ tell_start_T + off[k]`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BEAT_START_TICKS`, `BEAT_END_TICKS`, `BEAT_RAMP_TICKS` | int ticks | 150, 45, 1800 (`BEAT_RAMP_TICKS` = `TELL_MAX_TICKS`) | data | the opening interval, the closing interval and the ramp length |
| `BEAT_JITTER_TICKS`, `BEAT_MIN_GAP_TICKS` | int ticks | 8, 30 | data | the jitter amplitude and the gap floor between consecutive beats |
| `hash32` | pure integer function | 0 to 2^32 − 1 | ADR candidate shared with Lighting (LO:255) | **never `rng_derive`**; reconstructable from the persisted epoch |
| `k` | int | 0 and up | `tell_beat` | the beat index |
| `off[k]` | int ticks | 0 and up | block (`beat_next_off`) | the nominal offset from `tell_start_T` |

**Output Range:** intervals 37 to 158 ticks; about 20 beats in an 1,800-tick tell (the integral of `1 / interval` over 0 to 1800 is `(1800 / 105) × ln(150 / 45)` = 20.6). The schedule runs on **nominal** offsets, so run quantisation (a beat may publish up to five ticks late) never accumulates. **No beat is suppressed in the final gap before `breach_due_T`** (CQ-5).
**Floor proof (`MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` 4 in `[tell_start_T, tell_start_T + TELL_MIN_TICKS)`).** `off[k] + interval(off[k])` is strictly increasing in `off[k]` (slope about 0.94), so the latest `off[4]` comes from the largest jitter on every gap. Jitter 0: 0, 150, 292, 425, 551 (five beats; next 669). Every jitter +8: 0, 158, 307, 448, 580 (published on the run at +582, inside the window; next 705). Every jitter −8: 0, 142, 276, 402, 521 (five beats; next 633). So every legal `(tell_len ≥ 600, seed)` publishes at least **5** beats in the floor window against `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` 4: one beat of margin. An aborted tell is excluded from the sweep (it may end the block early). **Example:** epoch 12, `tell_start_T` 7,200: beat 0 publishes in the entry run at 7,200; beat 1 is due at offset 150 and publishes on the run at 7,350 (exact, since 150 is a multiple of 6); beat 4 with every jitter +8 is due at offset 580 and publishes at 7,782.

#### F-13. Tier

The tell_tier formula is defined as:

`tier = 0` outside TELL; inside, `tier = 1 + (e ≥ TIER2_TICKS) + (e ≥ TIER3_TICKS)` with `e = T − tell_start_T`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TIER2_TICKS`, `TIER3_TICKS` | int ticks | 600 and 1200 (safe 600 to 900 / 900 to 1500; `TIER2_TICKS` stays at least 600 while Lighting reads `tier ≥ 1`) | data | the elapsed thresholds |
| `e` | int ticks | 0 to `TELL_MAX_TICKS` | derived | the elapsed time |

**Output Range:** 0 to 3. The whole floor window is tier 1, Lighting's tier-1 gutter recipe (registry `gutter_recipe`, LO:257); every organic tell (`tell_len` ≥ 900) reaches tier 2, which the registry `gutter_recipe` spaces at 300/600 frames (placeholder). **Example:** the run at `e` = 594 shows tier 1; the run at `e` = 600 shows tier 2.

#### F-14. Abort

The tell_abort formula is defined as:

`below_hold_runs' = (L' < theta_HOLD) ? below_hold_runs + 1 : 0`; `abort = (below_hold_runs' × 6 ≥ ABORT_HOLD_TICKS) OR (T ≥ breach_due_T AND L' < theta_HOLD)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `theta_HOLD` | int64 mAU | `theta × HOLD_PM / 1000` | F-5 conventions | the hold line (R8's absolute definition stands; the baseline-relative form is an Open Question) |
| `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` | int ticks | 60 to 300, a multiple of 6; placeholder 120 | data | consecutive time under the hold line (20 runs; the first run under counts as run 1) |
| `below_hold_runs` | int | 0 to 50 | block (persisted) | the consecutive-run counter |

**Output Range:** boolean. If both clauses are true on one run, the abort wins (one transition). An abort deposits no STRESS, switches no beacon, sets `tell_outcome = ABORTED`, freezes `tell_beat`, leaves `L` unchanged when `ABORT_RELIEF_PM` is 1000 (else `L' = min(L', B + ABORT_RELIEF_PM × (theta − B) div 1000)`), and sets `rearm_until_T = T + TELL_REARM_TICKS`. **Example:** `L` falls under 4,800,000 at the run `T` = `tell_start_T` + 468; the 20th consecutive run under the line is at +582 and the tell aborts there (114 ticks of elapsed time, 20 runs counted).

#### F-15. Abortability invariant (registry: `abortability_invariant`)

The abortability_invariant formula is defined as:

`x_dark + (L_CEIL − x_dark) × (1 − 6 / tau)^n ≤ theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN`, with `n = (TELL_MIN_TICKS − 6) div 6`; the lint executes F-5 for `n` runs from `L_CEIL` with `I_run = 6 × r_dark` and asserts `L ≤ theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `x_dark` | int mAU | `B` to `theta_HOLD` | F-16 | the ring's survivable-dark steady level |
| `ATT_MARGIN` | int AU | 0 to 200; placeholder 50 | data | the proof's safety margin |
| `n` | int runs | 99 | derived from `TELL_MIN_TICKS` | runs available before `TELL_MIN_TICKS − 6` ticks elapse |
| `(1 − 6/tau)^n` | decay factor | 0.7185 at the placeholders | derived (continuous form 0.7189; the ceiling decay only falls faster, so this is the safe side) | what survives of the excess over `x_dark` |

**Output Range:** boolean per ring, and a cap on the dark level: `x_dark ≤ (theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN − 0.7185 × L_CEIL) / (1 − 0.7185)` = `(4,750 − 4,397) / 0.2815` = **1,253 AU**. Entry is assumed at `L_CEIL`, the highest the level can be (X1: during the refractory and the rearm window the level keeps integrating, and one run's maximum input has no design bound, so only a saturation makes the proof exist).
**Example (integer lint, no glows, F-16):** ring 1, `x_dark` 829 AU: the level falls below 4,750,000 mAU after **90** runs (540 ticks); ring 2 (979 AU): **93** runs (558 ticks); ring 3 (1,079 AU): **95** runs (570 ticks). All are at most 99 runs (594 ticks): holds, with 54, 36 and 24 ticks of slack. **With the two wake glows** (`N_GLOW_DARK` 2, `r_dark` 366, F-16): **93**, **96** and **98** runs (558, 576 and 588 ticks), slack 36, 18 and 6 ticks. *Sensitivity (the cost of depth, RK-2):*

| `tau` | decay over 594 ticks | `x_dark` cap (AU) | baseline cap after the 529 AU of dark load (AU) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 900 | 0.5169 | 3,284 | 2,755 |
| 1200 | 0.6096 | 2,611 | 2,082 |
| 1800 (ruled) | 0.7185 | 1,253 | 724 |

#### F-15b. Abortability, played range (registry: `abortability_play`)

The abortability_play formula is defined as:

`t_decay(ring) + REACT_ALLOW_TICKS ≤ TELL_DRAW_LO − 6`, with `t_decay(ring) = 6 × n_ring`, `n_ring` = the least `n` for which F-5 from `L_CEIL` at `I_run = 6 × r_dark` gives `L < theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_decay(ring)` | int ticks | 540 / 558 / 570 at rings 1 / 2 / 3 (558 / 576 / 588 with the two wake glows) | derived (closed form `tau × ln((L_CEIL − x_dark) / (theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN − x_dark))` = 540, 558, 570 to the run) | the dark decay time from the highest entry level |
| `REACT_ALLOW_TICKS` | int ticks | 120 to 300; placeholder 180 | data | the allowance between beat 0 and the player zeroing every input |
| `TELL_DRAW_LO` | int ticks | 900 | F-10 | the shortest tell a player meets in play |

**Output Range:** boolean per ring; slack = `TELL_DRAW_LO − 6 − (t_decay + REACT_ALLOW_TICKS)`. **Example:** ring 1: `540 + 180 = 720 ≤ 894`; ring 2: `558 + 180 = 738 ≤ 894`; ring 3: `570 + 180 = 750 ≤ 894`. All hold, with slack 174, 156 and 144 ticks (with the two wake glows: 738, 756 and 768, slack 156, 138 and 126). The early-abort path adds `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` (120) and still holds (660, 678, 690). **Moment A timeline check** (a player enters at `theta`, stops every input at once, ring 1, no glows): the level leaves `theta_HOLD` after **80** runs (480 ticks) and the 20th consecutive run under it is run 99, so the abort completes **594 ticks** after the player stops, against `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`; the resume beat survives by 6 ticks, so resume is the narrow favourable case and restart the common one. **Stated stance (moved here from Player Fantasy; answers the creative-director's CR-TIMELINE check):** the 594 ticks are 480 ticks to cross `theta_HOLD` from `theta` at ring 1 plus 114 ticks of `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` hold (the first run under the line counts as run 1, so the hold adds 19 runs), six inside `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`; an abort therefore usually forfeits the partial. That is a legitimate price of restraint, stated so it is a decision and not an accident, and it is balanced by a breach that has teeth (AT-11). The levers if a playtest wants the resume beat are `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` down and `HOLD_PM` up (Tuning Knobs).

#### F-16. Survivable-dark floor (registry: `survivable_dark_floor`)

The survivable_dark_floor formula is defined as:

`x_dark = B + r_dark × tau`, with `r_dark = (W_P × DARK_FLOOR_LOAD_W) div 1000 + (W_L[FIXTURE] × floor(output_pm_E × reach_px_E / 1000)) div 1000 + Σ_{g in N_GLOW_DARK} (W_L[FAULT_GLOW] × floor(output_pm_g × reach_px_g / 1000)) div 1000`; conditions (ii-a) `x_dark ≤ theta_HOLD − ATT_MARGIN` and (ii-b) F-15

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `r_dark` | int mAU per tick | 294 on a clean ship (150 + 144 + 0); 366 with the two wake glows | derived | everything the player cannot switch off, with the player still and the beam off |
| `DARK_FLOOR_LOAD_W` | int W | placeholder 1500 (of which 1000 W is the tier 0 and 1 branch, LSG:433) | the lint fixture: the home-ship's minimum survival load (O2, heat, reactor) with lamps off | the unswitchable power load; re-run when Assembly or the Graph changes the survival set |
| `E` | the cryo EMERGENCY lamp (1000, 144) | | DA-23 | the one `node_ref` 0 fixture; it counts as light (R5) but never carries a tell |
| `N_GLOW_DARK` | set of FAULT_GLOW emitters | **1 under the closed-valve staging, 2 under the OQ-DA-18 valve-OPEN fallback that ships until ASK-G1 is accepted** (DA-16: FUEL-DRIP's glow, plus O2-SHIP's 1/GAS glow only when `v_o2_cryo` is OPEN at wake; Derelict Authoring C-12, OQ-DA-35). The shipped rows therefore use **2**, the shipping value today, and are the conservative bound for the closed-valve staging; **0 for the clean-ship comparison**; the lint evaluates the count at every ring row | Derelict Authoring's fault list and Failure & Trail's glow table *(provisional)* | the unswitchable glows; a nominal glow (output 500, reach 72) adds 36 mAU per tick = 65 AU |

**Output Range:** clean ship (`N_GLOW_DARK` 0): ring 1: 829 AU; ring 2: 979 AU; ring 3: 1,079 AU (each extra unswitchable fixture adds 259 AU; each 100 W of survival load adds 18 AU; each nominal glow adds 65 AU). **The lint evaluates `N_GLOW_DARK` = the count of FAULT_GLOW emitters authored active at wake (two under the valve-OPEN fallback that ships until ASK-G1 is accepted, one under the closed-valve staging, DA-16) at every ring row**; at the placeholder `B_table` 300 / 450 / 550 it holds at 93 / 96 / 98 runs (at most 99). **Both bounds are checked** (CP-C8): (ii-a) is trivial (4,750 AU), and (ii-b) is the binding one (1,253 AU). **Example:** `x_dark(ring 1) = 300 + 270 + 259 = 829` AU, where 270 AU is `150 mAU/tick × 1800` and 259 AU is `144 × 1800`. **The glow case (RK-6, resolved by the placeholder rows):** two nominal glows (the fuel drip, plus the O2-SHIP gas glow that DA-16 makes active at wake only under the valve-OPEN fallback) add 72 mAU per tick, so `r_dark` = 366: ring 1 (959 AU) holds at 93 runs, ring 2 (1,109 AU) at 96 runs and ring 3 (1,209 AU) at 98 runs = 588 ticks, 6 ticks inside F-15 (it also passes F-15b: 588 + 180 = 768 ≤ 894). A ring-3 row of 600 AU needs 100 runs = 600 ticks with the two glows and fails F-15 by 6 ticks, which is why the placeholder row is 550; ring 3 can rise to about 594 AU with the two glows (595 fails) and to 724 AU on a clean ship, and a third nominal glow fails the 550 row (100 runs).

#### F-17. Breach end

The breach_end formula is defined as:

`end_BREACH = (D ≥ BREACH_MAX_TICKS) OR (D ≥ BREACH_MIN_TICKS AND clear_runs × 6 ≥ CLEAR_HOLD_TICKS) OR pod_departure_nonhome_locus OR (empty_nonhome_locus AND D ≥ BREACH_MIN_TICKS)`, with `D = T − breach_enter_T` and `clear_runs' = (D ≥ BREACH_MIN_TICKS AND L' < theta_HOLD) ? clear_runs + 1 : 0`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BREACH_MIN_TICKS` | int ticks | 900 (300 to 1800) | data | the shortest breach |
| `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` | int ticks | 3600 (1800 to 3899, bound by F-20) | data | the death-spiral guard |
| `CLEAR_HOLD_TICKS` | int ticks | 300 (60 to 900, a multiple of 6) | data | consecutive time under the hold line after the minimum |
| `pod_departure_nonhome_locus` | bool | | true when the locus body is not the home-ship, `locus_occupants_at_entry` is non-empty, and no living seat's `actor_section` is in `locus_body` (the last living occupant has left; a player who was never inside does not trigger it) *(provisional on Web Map's notification; derivable now from Body)* | the pod-hop clause (AT-22) |
| `empty_nonhome_locus` | bool | evaluated **at BREACH entry only** | `locus_occupants_at_entry` is empty AND the locus body is not the home-ship | the X10 clause (CQ-9) |

**Output Range:** a breach lasts at most 3,600 ticks; a pod departure from a non-home locus body can end it at any run, and the empty non-home body clause ends it at 900; the earliest end by quiet is `BREACH_MIN_TICKS + CLEAR_HOLD_TICKS − 6` = **1,194** ticks (the first counted run is the one at `D` = 900). **Example:** entry at `T` 20,004; the player flees to the pod and the level drops under `theta_HOLD` at `T` 20,400; counting starts at the run `T` 20,904 (`D` = 900) and the 50th consecutive run is `T` 21,198, which ends the breach (`D` = 1,194).

#### F-18. STRESS schedule

The stress_schedule formula is defined as:

`deposit at run q (q = runs since BREACH entry) iff phase == BREACH AND ( q < (S + STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN − 1) div STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN OR ( q ≥ STRESS_REFRESH_RUNS AND (q mod STRESS_REFRESH_RUNS) < STRESS_REFRESH_BURST_RUNS ) )`; each deposit run is M17 `deposit_trace(STRESS, s, magnitude = BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT)` for the next at most `STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` sections of `locus_body` in ascending `section_id`, from `stress_pending_cursor`, wrapping

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `S` | int | the number of sections of `locus_body` | recorded at TELL entry | the sections to cover |
| `STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` | int | 1 to 16; placeholder 8 | data | per-run deposit count (Horror's budget is at most `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP − STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN`, C22) |
| `STRESS_REFRESH_RUNS` | int runs | 100 to (`STRESS_HOLD_STEPS` − burst − margin); placeholder 300 | data | the re-arm period |
| `STRESS_REFRESH_BURST_RUNS` | int runs | 1 to 6; placeholder 3 | data | consecutive runs of one burst (tolerates a dropped deposit; M17 is idempotent) |
| `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS` | int steps | cited from the registry (`STRESS_HOLD_STEPS`, 600 steps; Graph LSG:526, :907) | the Graph | how long one deposit keeps STRESS armed |
| `BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT` | permille | cited from the registry (2000) | the Graph | the magnitude, passed by name |

**Output Range:** the coverage margin `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS − STRESS_REFRESH_RUNS − STRESS_REFRESH_BURST_RUNS` (297 steps = 29.7 s at the cited value) is positive by lint, and a second lint requires `STRESS_REFRESH_BURST_RUNS × STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN ≥ S_max` so one burst re-arms the whole body (24 ≥ 14, the MVP's largest body: home ship 5, derelict A 4 and derelict B 5 compartments). STRESS lapses `STRESS_HOLD_STEPS × 6` ticks after the last honoured deposit, so the **tail** past SUBSIDING's end is `2,400 − (BREACH end − last deposit)` ticks, 606 to 2,400. No deposit is made on or after the run that ends BREACH. **Example:** `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` 3,600 (600 runs), a locus body of 5 sections: all five are covered at `q` = 0; a burst at `q` = 300, 301 and 302; the breach ends at `q` = 600; the last deposit (`q` 302) is 1,788 ticks before the end, so STRESS lapses 3,600 ticks after it, which is 612 ticks after SUBSIDING ends. For a breach that ends early at 1,194 ticks the tail is 1,206 ticks.

#### F-19. Subside reset

The subside_reset formula is defined as:

`L' = min(L, B + (POST_PM × (theta − B)) div 1000)` at SUBSIDING entry

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `POST_PM` | int permille | 200 to 600; placeholder 400 | data | the residual fraction of the span kept after a breach |
| `SUBSIDE_TICKS` | int ticks | 600 to 2400; placeholder 1200 | data | the valley length |

**Output Range:** `B` to `theta`; the level is cut to a residual, never wiped. **Example:** `B` 300,000: `300,000 + (400 × 5,700,000) div 1000 = 2,580,000` (2,580 AU). A player who keeps running with the beam on from that residual re-crosses `theta` 1,782 ticks after the reset, which is 582 ticks into the 1,800-tick refractory (the refractory starts when SUBSIDING's 1,200 ticks end), so it is the refractory, not the level, that holds the next tell off.

#### F-20. Event-defer invariant (registry: `event_defer_invariant`)

The event_defer_invariant formula is defined as:

`TELL_MAX_TICKS + BREACH_MAX_TICKS + SUBSIDE_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS < AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TELL_MAX_TICKS`, `SUBSIDE_TICKS`, `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` | int ticks | 1800, 1200, 3600 | this GDD | the three phase caps |
| `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` | int ticks | cited (registry; 300) | Save/Load | one retry of the soft gate |
| `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` | int ticks | cited (registry; 7200) | Save/Load | the deferral cap past which a save is RISKY |

**Output Range:** boolean. **Example:** `1800 + 3600 + 1200 + 300 = 6900 < 7200` (margin 300 ticks, one retry). The inequality binds `BREACH_MAX_TICKS < 3,900` at the other placeholders; at 3,900, one above the range top, it fails (AC-AT-51's negative fixture). Re-check across every `gate_tell_mode` (FAIR and STRICT) whenever any term changes.

#### F-21. Rearm and refractory against the retry

The rearm_refract formula is defined as:

`TELL_REARM_TICKS ≥ AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` and `REFRACT_TICKS ≥ AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TELL_REARM_TICKS` | int ticks | `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` to 1800; placeholder 600 | data | the gap after an abort |
| `REFRACT_TICKS` | int ticks | `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` to 3600; placeholder 1800 | data | the gap after a breach |

**Output Range:** boolean. An open soft-gate window exists between chained aborted tells and between chained events, even in STRICT mode. **Example:** `600 ≥ 300` and `1800 ≥ 300`: hold.

#### F-22. Noise unit relations (registry: `noise_unit_relations`)

The noise_unit_relations formula is defined as:

IR-1 `2 ≤ U_TOOL ≤ 6`; IR-2 `U_LOUD ≥ 30 × U_TOOL`; IR-3 `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS ≤ 2 × TAU_ATT_TICKS`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `U_TOOL` | int AU | 2 to 6; placeholder 4 | this GDD | one accrued tool-channel tick |
| `U_LOUD` | int AU | `30 × U_TOOL` to 400; placeholder 180 (= 30 × 6) | this GDD | one-shot at cut completion, seam-cut DONE, vent apply, throw landing |
| `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` | int ticks | cited (registry; 600) | Interaction | how long a partial job is kept |

**Output Range:** boolean ×3, handed over by Interaction (IA-33). 180 holds IR-2 across the **whole** legal `U_TOOL` range (at `U_TOOL` 6, `30 × 6 = 180`), not only at the default. **Example:** `2 ≤ 4 ≤ 6`; `180 ≥ 120`; `600 ≤ 3600` (a job split across the TTL retains `e^(−600/1800)` = 72 percent).

#### F-23. The tell-window contract (registry: `tell_window_contract`)

The tell_window_contract formula is defined as:

for every recorded harm event `e` at tick `t_e` in section `s_e`: `EXISTS epoch k: tell_start_k + tell_len_k ≤ t_e < subside_end_k AND tell_len_k ≥ TELL_MIN_TICKS AND s_e in locus_body_k`; companion floor: for every epoch, at least `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` beats are published in `[tell_start_k, tell_start_k + TELL_MIN_TICKS)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `e` | harm event | web-originated M17 RUPTURE, GOUGE, STRESS; environmental M18 `apply_damage(…, EXOGENOUS)`; **provisionally** a lethal act by a horror on a crew body | the `TellAudit` ledger (DIAGNOSTIC, a test fixture first) | what is audited |
| `t_e` | tick | | `T` of the call | when it happened |
| `tell_start_k`, `tell_len_k`, `subside_end_k` | ticks | | the epoch record | the epoch's window |
| `locus_body_k` | set of section ids | | the epoch's locus body in force at `t_e`: recorded at TELL entry and replaced only by an AT-14 fallback, which TellAudit records as `LOCUS_FALLBACK(epoch, old_body, new_body, tick)` | the sections the breach may harm |
| `MIN_BEATS_IN_FLOOR` | int | 3 to 5; placeholder 4 | data | the companion floor |

**Output Range:** boolean over a replay; **0 violations** required. Player-originated acts (cut, vent) are not events. The Graph's endogenous gate is a different rule. **Example:** a STRESS deposit at `T` 8,244 against epoch 12 (`tell_start` 7,200, `tell_len` 1,044, BREACH from 8,244 for 3,600 ticks, so `subside_end` = 8,244 + 3,600 + 1,200 = 13,044): `7,200 + 1,044 = 8,244 ≤ 8,244 < 13,044`, `1,044 ≥ 600`, section in the body: passes. A stub that deposits at `T` 8,238 fails (one run early); a burst deposit at 13,044 fails (not `< subside_end`).

#### F-24. Pre-tell cue step and the cue-floor calibration lint

The ambient_cue_step formula is defined as:

`cue_step = 0` if `level_pm < AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM`, else `min(CUE_STEPS, 1 + ((level_pm − AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM) × CUE_STEPS) div (1000 − AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM))`; a step falls only when `level_pm < rise_edge − CUE_HYST_PM`; lint: `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM ≥ max over rings of walk_beam_pm(ring) + CUE_MARGIN_PM`, with `walk_beam_pm(ring) = (1000 × r_wb × tau) div (theta − B_ring)` and `r_wb = U[WALK] × 1000 + W_L[BEAM] × beam_term div 1000`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AMBIENT_CUE_FLOOR_PM` | int permille | 200 to 650; **derived default 550** | data; Tuning Knobs | below it no cue plays |
| `CUE_STEPS`, `CUE_HYST_PM`, `CUE_MARGIN_PM` | int | 4, 25, 100 (safe 3 to 6, 10 to 50, 50 to 200) | data | the quantisation, the hysteresis and the calibration margin |
| `r_wb` | int mAU per tick | 1288 | derived: walking 1 AU (cited `NOISE_PER_TICK` walk) = 1000 mAU plus the wide beam's EM term 288 (EM D.12 example, wide beam (1000, 288)) at weight 1000 | the walk-with-beam inflow |
| `walk_beam_pm(ring)` | int permille | 406 / 417 / 425 at rings 1 / 2 / 3 | derived | the steady `level_pm` of a player walking with the beam on |

**Output Range:** `cue_step` 0 to 4, no value between steps; at floor 550 the rise edges are 550, 663, 775 and 888 (and the falls 525, 638, 750 and 863). **CP-C2 arithmetic (integers):** the walk-with-beam steady level is `B + 1,288 × 1,800` = `300,000 + 2,318,400` = **2,618,400 mAU (2,618 AU)** at ring 1, so `walk_beam_pm` = `(1000 × 2,318,400) div 5,700,000` = **406**; ring 2: `div 5,550,000` = 417; ring 3 (`B` 550,000): `div 5,450,000` = **425**. The floor is `425 + CUE_MARGIN_PM 100 = 525`, rounded up to the next multiple of 50 = **550**. The seed value 350 fails the lint at every ring (350 < 406) and is superseded (C23). **Example:** `level_pm` 473 gives step 0 (below 550); 700 gives step 2 (700 − 550 = 150; 150 × 4 div 450 = 1; 1 + 1 = 2); 1000 clamps to step 4. *Lint scope, stated so it is a decision:* the trace is the player's own spend (walk plus beam). Adding the unswitchable dark load (294 mAU per tick) gives steady levels of 499 / 513 / 522 permille, still under 550; adding the home ship's three lit fixtures and idle power (766 mAU per tick) gives 648 at ring 1, step 1: a player who walks with the beam on in a derelict while the home ship burns its lights hears the ordinary hull-settling creaks, which is the intended diegetic reading of "the lights you left on" (open: OQ-AT-14).

#### F-25. Design aids (not rules): steady states, crossing times, the felt-target table

The steady_state_aids formula is defined as:

`L_ss = B + r × tau`; crossing time `t = tau × ln((L_ss − L0) / (L_ss − theta))`; decay-to-line time `t = tau × ln((L0 − x_dark) / (theta_HOLD − x_dark))`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `r` | mAU per tick | 0 to about 6,000 | the sum of the stimulus rates | a constant inflow |
| `L0` | mAU | `B` to `L_CEIL` | the start level | where the trace begins |
| `x_dark` | mAU | F-16 | F-16 | the decay target when every input stops |

**Output Range:** design aids for the calibration harness, never read by the sim (closed-form oracles; the integer model agrees within 3 percent, the game-designer's GD-F1 tolerance). **Example (scenario table at the placeholders, `B` 300 AU, integer-model checked):**

| Situation | `r` (mAU/tick) | `L_ss` (AU) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Home ship lit and idle (three fixtures, the EMERGENCY lamp, 1,900 W) | 576 + 190 = 766 | 1,679 | `level_pm` 241 |
| Dark (survival load and the EMERGENCY lamp) | 150 + 144 = 294 | 829 | the F-16 floor |
| Walking with the beam in a derelict, home ship lit | 766 + 1000 + 288 = 2,054 | 3,997 | `level_pm` 648; under `theta_HOLD` |
| Running with the beam, home ship lit | 766 + 4000 + 288 = 5,054 | 9,397 | crosses `theta` from idle in 1,476 ticks (24.6 s) |
| Tool channel with the beam (a cut) | 5,054 | 9,397 | same as running; the lit-only rule (IA-6) means a tool always carries light |
| Breaker cold start (`INRUSH_PM` 1500) | 54,000 mAU per run for five runs | +270 AU | the hiding verb has a price |
| Moment A (enter at `theta`, stop everything) | 294 | | leaves `theta_HOLD` in 480 ticks; abort at 594 (F-15b) |

*Felt-target rationale (CQ-6, CQ-7).* The game-designer derived the weights from felt AU-per-tick targets: walk 1.0 and run 4.0 (cited), tool 4.0, chest beam about 0.58, a live glowstick about 0.29 (four live: 1.15, more than a walk), a powered fixture about 0.10 (five at rest add 0.5), surplus survival draw about 0.15 per kilowatt, one breaker close 150 to 300 AU, the dark-still floor about 0.30; this document keeps the single dimension above (beam 0.29, glowstick 0.10, fixture 0.14, surplus 0.15 per 1,500 W) because it reproduces R4's calibration and has no per-kind fudge, and the felt-target column is the **playtest alternative** (weights about 2000 for `BEAM` and 3000 for `DEPLOYABLE`, 700 for fixtures). *Alternative placeholder set (CQ-6):* the game-designer's K1 (`ATT_THRESHOLD` 7200 AU, `U_LOUD` 600) lengthens the reckless crossing from 24.6 s to 37.7 s (integer F-5 run from the home-idle level, 2,262 ticks) and leaves the AC-AT-57f chain crossing at about 50 s (the same fifth job end), at the cost of a larger `theta − B` span; both sets satisfy IR-2. *Occupancy:* for a player who never goes below the hold line, `calm_fraction = REFRACT_TICKS / (tell_mean + BREACH_MAX_TICKS + SUBSIDE_TICKS + REFRACT_TICKS)` = 1,800 / (1,350 + 3,600 + 1,200 + 1,800) = 22.6 percent, inside the 15 to 35 percent calm-time target (and above AC-AT-80's 20 percent floor) for riding every tell; the levers are `REFRACT_TICKS` 3600 (36.9 percent) and `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` 2700 (25.5 percent), a playtest trigger rather than a rule.

## Edge Cases

*Each entry reads "If [condition]: [exact outcome]. [rationale]" and names the diagnostic or reason code where one exists. Entries are grouped by theme; `E-n` ids are stable references for the Acceptance Criteria. Rule ids are `AT-n`, formulas `F-n`. Provisional entries say which sibling or undesigned system they depend on.*

#### Intake, level and bounds

- **E-1. If `push_stimulus` is called with an unknown `class` or a `kind` other than `NOISE`**: it adds nothing, increments the unknown-class counter and returns; the call never rejects or asserts (Class B: clamp, never reject; AT-3). Reserved kinds are rejected by lint at authoring, not at run time.
- **E-2. If a `QUIET` push arrives**: nothing is added to `pending_mAU` or any ledger and the `QUIET` counter increments. The class exists so Interaction can declare a silent verb explicitly (IA-33).
- **E-3. If a push's source resolves to a dormant or unknown section** (an object in a cut-away section, a foot origin in no section): the push still counts toward `I_noise` and so toward `L`, is dropped from the ledger, and `dropped_unattributed` increments (F-2). The web hears a sound it cannot place; the level is the world's and the locus is only a choice.
- **E-4. If many pushes stack in one run** (six seats each pushing `LOUD`): `pending_mAU[src]` saturates at `L_CEIL` per source, `I_run` is bounded by (sources × `L_CEIL`), and `L'` clamps at `L_CEIL`; all arithmetic stays far below 2^63 (F-1, F-5). The saturation changes nothing below the ceiling.
- **E-5. If `power_use` is absent for a loaded section**: `I_power[s]` is 0, the diagnostic `ATT_POWER_ABSENT` is logged once per process, and `served_pm` is **not** substituted (it already enters as light). Nothing else changes (F-4).
- **E-6. If `emitters()` is empty, every section is dormant or no section is loaded**: `I_light` and `I_power` are 0, no ledger exists, `L` decays to `B` exactly (F-5), and a TELL cannot start (lint: `B < theta` at every ring).
- **E-7. If 96 emitters (`MAX_EMITTERS`) are live**: `I_light` is at most 221,184 mAU per run at weight 1000 and 884,736 at the weight cap (F-3), and `L` pins at `L_CEIL`; a TELL then starts at the first legal run. The MVP slice (five home compartments) cannot reach this; a Vertical Slice growth that does (about 29 powered fixtures reach the hold line at rest at the game-designer's K1 values) is a tripwire for the lint `survivable_dark_floor`, which fails at authoring, and for the open question on per-section lamp switching (GD-15). "Lights you left on" must stay a decision and not become an inevitability.
- **E-8. If a ring change raises `B` above `L`** (descent): `L` is lifted to `B` in the same run (`dec` is 0 because `L − B` is negative), `level_pm` reads 0, and no TELL starts unless `L ≥ theta`. The held-ring rule (AT-6) applies while a seat is `IN_POD`.
- **E-9. If `B` falls** (a return to the home ring): `L` decays toward the new `B` at a rate proportional to the excess; there is no free reset, because the level is absolute and the baseline only a floor.
- **E-10. If `theta ≤ B` for any ring, or a `B_table` row breaks F-15 or F-16**: boot refuses (the data lint of AT-6, evaluated with `N_GLOW_DARK` = the glows authored active at wake, F-16). A division by zero in `level_pm` and a ring on which restraint cannot end a tell are both data errors caught before play.
- **E-11. If `L` equals `theta_HOLD` exactly**: it is not below the line (the comparison is strict `<`), so no abort counting happens and, on the due run, the breach commits. The boundary is deterministic and chosen to favour consequence over relief.

#### Simultaneous rules and timing boundaries

- **E-12. If a `LOUD` push lands in the run that crosses `theta`**: it is part of that run's `I_run`; the run enters TELL, draws `tell_len ≥ TELL_DRAW_LO`, and publishes beat 0 in the same run. The breach cannot occur before `tell_start_T + tell_len`; there is no instant-breach path (AT-8).
- **E-13. If two crossings occur in one run** (two `LOUD` pushes in co-op): one TELL, one `tell_epoch` increment and one RNG draw (the counter advances by exactly 1).
- **E-14. If `L ≥ theta` while `T < refract_until_T` or `T < rearm_until_T`**: the phase stays CALM, `L` keeps integrating up to `L_CEIL`, `level_pm` reads 1000, and the first run at or after the later timer enters TELL with beat 0 and `tell_elapsed` 0. No catch-up elapsed time is credited, so a long wait never shortens the tell.
- **E-15. If TELL entry falls exactly on `T == refract_until_T`** (or `rearm_until_T`): it is legal (the guard is `≥`).
- **E-16. If the abort counter reaches `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` on the same run as `T ≥ breach_due_T`**: the abort wins and is one transition (`tell_outcome = ABORTED`, no STRESS, no beacon). A run that is both "due" and "under the line" is an abort, never a breach.
- **E-17. If `L` drops below `theta_HOLD` and rises back before `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` elapses**: `below_hold_runs` resets to 0; only the at-due path remains. One dip does not abort.
- **E-18. If the clear-hold clause and `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` are both satisfied on one run**: one transition, BREACH to SUBSIDING; deposits stop on that run (F-18).
- **E-19. If `attention_arm_tell` is called outside CALM**: it is rejected with reason `NOT_CALM` and changes nothing. Inside CALM it enters TELL at the next run, clamps `tell_len` to `[TELL_MIN_TICKS, TELL_MAX_TICKS]` (100 becomes 600), does not honour the two timers and does not consume `rng_counter` (F-9, F-10).
- **E-20. If the beam is strobed in step with the six-tick cadence**: light is sampled once per run, so the sampled state can be systematically off. The Controller adapter's toggle debounce (EM:720) owns the defence; Attention adds no cooldown and no sim-side rule (the EM text says the sim adds none). A person cannot phase-lock to a sample tick they cannot see; only a macro could, and a macro strobing the beam off at every sample is paying the lit-only verb price (IA-6) for nothing.

#### Tell, abort and the pod

- **E-21. If a player is DOWNED during a TELL**: the tell continues; their noise stops (a downed seat pushes nothing); their beam is whatever the Emitter State reports; a DOWNED seat counts as living for the baseline ring and the pod hold (AT-31). The tell is world-level, never player-level.
- **E-22. If every living player dies during a TELL**: the death stub restores the newest verified autosave (SL-7) and Attention rolls back with it (WORLD scope), including `rng_counter`, `tell_len` and every timer, so the restored run replays the same tell length and beat sequence with no reroll.
- **E-23. If a player hops by pod during a TELL**: the tell clock continues and `held_ring` keeps `B` until arrival. Under LS-17's default (a left-behind derelict is dormant), which Web Map & Transit adopts (a departed derelict is dormant at once, WM:102, WM-26), the departed locus section goes dormant, so the locus falls back per AT-14 and E-27 to the home ship's lowest `section_id` body, and the due-run breach STRESSes that body (the ruled R14 behaviour; because it can move the breach onto the escaping crew it is recorded for the creative-director at OQ-AT-12). Only if Web Map keeps the departed derelict loaded does the breach STRESS the recorded `locus_body`, with the X10 clause ending it at `BREACH_MIN_TICKS` (F-17). Departure during a TELL never aborts it.
- **E-24. If a tell is the shortest possible** (`attention_arm_tell` with 600): the beats follow F-12 (at least 5 in the window), the tier follows F-13, and the BREACH-or-abort decision still occurs at the due run. R10 (b) binds at this length, and at ring 3 with the placeholder dark load it holds with 24 ticks of slack, 6 with the two wake glows (F-15).
- **E-25. If the player enters TELL at the highest possible level and does everything right** (stops, kills the lamp, opens the breaker): the abort completes inside the shortest tell in play (F-15b slack 144 to 174 ticks, 126 at ring 3 with the two wake glows) but only if the reaction allowance holds; a player who reacts after `REACT_ALLOW_TICKS` forfeits the abort on the shortest draws. That is the stated price of attention, not a defect (AT-10).
- **E-26. If the player stops everything at `theta` and later tries to resume the interrupted cut**: the abort completes 594 ticks after the stop (F-15b), 6 ticks inside `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`; any reaction delay beyond that forfeits the partial, and the cut restarts from zero. Restraint costing the partial is a legitimate price (AT stance on Moment A).

#### Breach, locus, STRESS and the beacon

- **E-27. If the locus section is cut away or goes dormant during TELL or BREACH** *(provisional — Assembly and Web Map)*: the locus falls back to the home-ship's lowest `section_id`, `locus_body` is recomputed, the tell is not restarted and `tell_remaining` is unchanged; TellAudit records `LOCUS_FALLBACK(epoch, old_body, new_body, tick)` and the contract evaluates later deposits against the new body (F-23, AT-15); STRESS already armed on a cut-away section lapses on its own hold. **A cut moves the locus and never launders Attention** (Modular Ship Assembly E-26, T15 A1): the level `L`, `phase`, the timers and `tell_epoch` are unchanged by the cut, and only the cut-away section's ledger entry is dropped. In BREACH, a dormancy caused by a pod departure that empties a non-home locus is resolved by the AT-22 departure test first (it ends the breach, WM:87), so the fallback applies only when the breach is not ended that run.
- **E-28. If two sections tie in `ledger_mAU` at TELL entry**: the lowest `section_id` is the locus (a deterministic tie-break, F-6).
- **E-29. If a STRESS deposit is dropped because `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` is saturated in the run** (Horror deposits by path (a), Class B `deposit_trace` from the `horror.step` hook, so Attention claims no first place, C22, Horror HE-28): Attention cannot observe the drop (the mutator is Class B and returns no rejection), so the section is unstressed until the next burst run, one burst period (`STRESS_REFRESH_RUNS` × 6 = 1,800 ticks) later at most. Missing STRESS is never an unannounced harm (STRESS only amplifies failures that were already ticking), so the tell-window contract is not violated; the guarantee against the drop is Horror's must-repeat budget of at most `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP − STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN` per run.
- **E-30. If `set_beacon` is rejected** (`NOT_LIVE` because the home-ship's power is dead, `NO_SUCH_EMITTER`, `NOT_BEACON`) or expires at `max_age_ticks`: the machine proceeds (the bed, STRESS and the failing hardware carry the breach), the failure is logged once, and the command is retried once per run while `beacon_retry_left > 0` (`BEACON_RETRY_RUNS` placeholder 20). A dead ship's beacon is dark (EM:200); this is intended.
- **E-31. If the beacon's own draw reaches the power channel in BREACH or SUBSIDING**: both power terms exclude the commanded beacon's watts (F-4), so the alarm cannot hold `L` above `theta_HOLD` and delay the exit that it is tested against. If the Graph or Emitter owner answers that `lamps_lit_w` already includes the beacon, the double subtraction under-counts by at most the beacon's watts (12 mAU per run at the beacon's 20 W, DA-24): a lint finding to resolve, never a behaviour change.
- **E-32. If the beacon is switched on at BREACH entry and the listener or the nearest player is on a derelict**: no visible beacon reaches them (one beacon, on the home-ship, DA-24); the structure-borne bed and the failing hardware carry the breach, and under reduced flashing the beacon is a static mark (AT-18, AT-24).
- **E-33. If a breach's locus body is a non-home body with no living player at BREACH entry** (Moment C, the lights left on; X10): BREACH ends at `BREACH_MIN_TICKS`; STRESS lapses on the Graph's hold; the beacon goes off at SUBSIDING entry. The clause is evaluated at entry only, so a player who arrives later is not retroactively rescued and, not being in `locus_occupants_at_entry`, does not trigger clause (iii) by leaving; clause (iii) is for an occupant recorded at entry.
- **E-34. If STRESS outlasts SUBSIDING** (tail of 606 to 2,400 ticks, F-18): no new Attention exogenous call occurs in CALM (the contract predicate is satisfied by the existing arming), and the ship keeps failing faster for the tail. This is the intended "spreading wrongness, then silence" (LSG:986); no Attention surface shows the tail and `phase == CALM` is never an all-clear (Audio must repeat).
- **E-35. If a breach lands on a clean ship** (no endogenous fault in the locus body): STRESS multiplies nothing and the breach is nearly a non-event. This cannot be the normal MVP case: every possible locus body on MVP derelicts A and B is authored with at least one active overload or fatigue fault (AT-11, the gate's content requirement; AC-AT-117), and Horror's single MVP behaviour wired to BREACH is a blocking dependency. A body the player has repaired clean is their earned result, and `BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT` is not raised to hide it.
- **E-36. If the cryo bay (the EMERGENCY fixture `fx_cryo_lamp`) holds the locus or the player is in it during a tell**: the lamp counts as light in F-3, never gutters and never carries a tier (LO-20, DA-23); filaments and the audio bed carry the tell, and no outcome changes.
- **E-37. If the locus body has more sections than one burst can cover** (`S > STRESS_REFRESH_BURST_RUNS × STRESS_DEPOSITS_PER_RUN`): the second lint of F-18 fails at authoring; at run time the cursor wraps and the remaining sections wait for the next burst. The MVP's largest body (14) is covered by 24.

#### Degenerate strategies

- **E-38. If the player stands still in the dark indefinitely** (CR-DOM): the level settles at `x_dark` (F-16), no tell can start, and nothing progresses, because every repair, cut and weld verb is lit-only (IA-6), the loops run on the survival load that is part of `x_dark`, and the breaker's inrush is the price of ever lighting up again. The web is not toothless: the cost of restraint is time, air and heat, not an Attention penalty. A playtest tripwire (abort share above 85 percent among experienced players, AC-GD-16) is the signal that the freeze has become a ritual.
- **E-39. If a player baits tells and aborts them for free** (tell farming): an abort leaves `L` unchanged (`ABORT_RELIEF_PM` 1000), so the next crossing costs the same effort as the first, and `TELL_REARM_TICKS` forbids re-entry for 600 ticks. The knob `ABORT_RELIEF_PM` below 1000 exists only as a lever if playtests show chained-abort fatigue.
- **E-40. If a player throws a glowstick into an empty section to steer the locus** (CR-LURE): the stick adds section emission (EM:722) and a `LOUD` landing push (IA-33), so the global level rises and the ledger of that section rises with it; the locus may land there. Allowed (light is a spend; the global level also rose); a playtest watch item together with CR-DOM, and `LOCUS_OCC_PREF_PM` is the lever.
- **E-41. If a player splits a long job across pauses to launder noise**: IR-3 keeps `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` at most twice `TAU_ATT_TICKS`, so a job split across the TTL retains about 72 percent of its first block; there is no pause length that resets the level to `B` except the decay itself.
- **E-42. If a player skims the threshold** (repeated entry and abort at the edge): the 600-tick rearm and the unchanged level make each cycle cost a full decay and a full re-approach; onset cues cannot chatter (AUD X10 adopted).
- **E-43. If a player runs continuously with the beam on**: the level crosses `theta` from `B` in 2,412 ticks (40 s) in the integer model and the tell begins; this is the intended punishment for refusing the one noise readout the game gives (the footsteps).
- **E-44. If an Assembly weld completes in a derelict welded to the home ship**: **zero** `LOUD` is pushed at weld completion (IA-33 declares `LOUD` one-shots at cut completion, seam-cut DONE, vent apply and throw landing only; a weld completion is none of these; the weld's cost to Attention is its accrued `TOOL` ticks), Assembly pushes nothing at MVP, and the locus body now spans both, so the next breach shares STRESS across the welded body. A seam-cut reaching DONE pushes exactly one `LOUD`, from Interaction (Modular Ship Assembly T14 C5).

#### Save, restore and persistence

- **E-45. If a snapshot is restored mid-TELL**: `rng_counter`, `tell_len`, `breach_due_T`, `tell_beat`, `beat_next_off` and `below_hold_runs` restore, no length is redrawn, no onset cue replays (cues fire on transitions only), and the beat sequence is hash-identical to uninterrupted play (G4; AT-1, AT-27).
- **E-46. If a snapshot is restored mid-BREACH**: `stress_next_burst_run` and `stress_pending_cursor` restore, bursts resume on schedule, and no deposit gap exceeds the coverage margin; the beacon is reconciled by the first run (E-48).
- **E-47. If a snapshot lands on the `breach_due_T` run**: both the pre-run state (phase TELL) and the post-run state (phase BREACH) restore to an identical continuation, because the transition is inside one run and `phase` changes nowhere else.
- **E-48. If `beacon_commanded` disagrees with the emitter's switch state after a restore** (a SYSTEM command pending at snapshot time is dropped on reload, KRN:593, :609): `post_restore` changes nothing and submits nothing; the first `attention.step` run after the restore compares the two and submits `set_beacon` only on a mismatch, with the usual `BEACON_RETRY_RUNS` retry. If they already agree, nothing is submitted (idempotent).
- **E-49. If a save made in CALM with `tell_outcome = ABORTED`, or in BREACH, is loaded**: no onset, no breach-enter and no release cue plays (transition-only); the view carries the persisted `tell_outcome` and `tell_epoch`.
- **E-50. If a loaded block holds an out-of-range value, an incoherent phase or an unknown section id**: restore returns a hard failure with the reason `ATTENTION_RESTORE_RANGE`, `_PHASE_INCOHERENT` or `_SECTION_UNKNOWN`; nothing is clamped and the load is refused (SL-15).
- **E-51. If an autosave is due during a TELL**: Save/Load's gate reads `tell_active`, `tell_remaining` and `breach_phase`; because `breach_phase` reports TELL as CALM, FAIR mode stays live (C5). A state saved mid-TELL restores the full tell (`tell_len ≥ TELL_MIN_TICKS`), so the player is never saved into a shorter warning than the contract allows. The gate formula itself is Save/Load's.
- **E-52. If the game is paused or the window loses focus**: sim `T` stops and Attention runs on `T` only; no wall time is read, so no catch-up burst of beats or decay occurs on resume (AT-28).

#### Hallucination interplay (the tell is a truth channel)

- **E-53. If a phantom entity lunges at a low-sanity player** (class a) with no beat, no resolving filament and no beacon before it: TellAudit records nothing (there is no web-originated event), Attention does nothing, and the player has a cheap first check that costs attention (they must have been listening to the bed): "the web did not look up, so this is not the web" (AT-17). The boundary is honest: it rules out web-originated harm, not an entity's presence and not the crew; a real entity may stand in CALM (Horror's non-lethal presence outside BREACH is required), so the check never licenses ignoring what is seen.
- **E-54. If a false system state shows a hull failing** (class b) while `phase` is CALM: no STRESS exists, the real gauge is unaffected, and `attention.view` still reports CALM; Attention reads no `PERCEPTION_OUT` value, so the lie cannot reach the machine (CR-12).
- **E-55. If the Perception Filter or Sanity tries to remove, add, delay or alter a tell cue** (a filament, a bed beat): it is a lint failure (the Filter's write set must not contain an Attention cue; its anchor list includes the tell cues). The MVP has no phantom tell and no false calm (AT-17).
- **E-56. If a crewmate appears as a horror and lethal friendly harm follows** (class c): friendly harm is Sanity's gate, not Attention's; Attention neither gates nor enables it. A crewmate's tool harm is player-originated and needs no tell; only a horror's lethal act on a crew body is audited by `tell_window_contract`, provisionally (R15).
- **E-57. If a phantom emitter, a hallucinated beam or phantom loot exists in the player's view**: the emitter list holds real records only (EM-3 gives a record no phantom field), so phantom light adds nothing; taking phantom loot picks nothing up and pushes no noise. Real carry noise comes only from a real action.

#### Co-op hooks (Vertical Slice; one machine at MVP)

- **E-58. If two players stand in different rings** (VS-B): `B` uses the maximum ring over living players (F-8); each hears the non-positional bed; `tell_beat` is shared (`SHARED_SUMMARY`); the single level and the `source` tag keep per-player attribution a data change.
- **E-59. If a downed crewmate's beam or a corpse could be a lantern**: noted only (EM:1121); it counts as an emitter if the Emitter State lists it, with no Attention rule of its own.
- **E-60. If the locus body holds only some of the players**: STRESS and `exogenous_permitted` apply to the locus body alone; the other players still hear the bed (perceivable relative to every living player, AT-15) and the home-ship beacon, and are safe from the breach's STRESS unless their body is welded to the locus body.

#### Absent or provisional neighbours

- **E-61. If Web Map's ring is absent**: `ring_of` (registry, Web Map & Transit) resolves an absent or sub-1 ring to ring 1 with one log line per process (AT-6).
- **E-62. If Ship Body's `actor_section` is unavailable for a seat** *(provisional — Body is a sibling in flight)*: that player's noise enters `I_noise` and the ledger drop of E-3 applies; the foot-origin lookup never blocks the machine.
- **E-63. If the Controller's `loco_state` is DEAD for every seat**: no section "contains a living player", so `B` holds its last value; the death handling then restores an autosave (E-22). Attention never invents a ring.
- **E-64. If the crew is aboard a derelict while the home ship stays lit** (Boarding & Salvage OQ-BS-15, AC-BS-80): the home ship's light and power terms **keep feeding the one world level** (AT-2; R2). A HOME-kind section is never dormant (Web Map & Transit), so its emitters and `power_use` are read every run like any loaded section's ("off-screen and unoccupied emitters count", EM:247); the lit-home term (766 mAU per tick at the F-25 placeholders) is part of the level the crew carries on the derelict. Switching home lamps off before a sortie is the player's lever. The locus can be a home section if its ledger leads (AT-14, Moment C).

The three downstream dependents of this run (Horror Entities, Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Adaptive Audio & Tells) and Web Map & Transit, Modular Ship Assembly, Perception Filter, Boarding & Salvage, HUD & Suit Layer, Instruments, Chart Table and Debug Overlay have no GDD: every row about them is provisional.*
